# C92: an exact signed tail rule and its complete asymptotic class limit

Status: **UNVERIFIED joint proof draft**. This is actual runtime launch
round 2, cumulative round 23 after baseline 21, under the unchanged cap 23.
The previous runtime round contained both C90 and C91; its internal-stage
round count was corrected in finding `92d17fda8c9a843f`. No numerical
evaluation, scan, replay, solver, installation, subordinate agent, or
verifier call is used here.

Accepted mathematical predecessors are `1252b8823bb5a290` (all-pair smooth
convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate), `0e7e78056dc6258c`
(the scalar moment elimination), `b98bb807d514e80d` (the original benchmark
and exact single-cut example), and `04219554e41d9750` (the original dyadic
benchmark limit). No unverified C90 or C91 assertion is a proof premise.
All new assertions used below are proved together in this document.

The original arbitrary-schedule problem remains open. The result below
settles the strength of one dense signed geometry, not the original problem.

## 1. Original schedule, class, and claims

For each integer m>=4 set

    N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,
    c=sigma(1+1/m), a=delta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

Use exactly h=H_m, so h_t=1+a*c^(nu_2(t+1)) for 0<=t<n.
All occurrences of a and c retain the enclosing m. Put

    ell=1+a=h_0=h_(n-1), S=sum_(t<n)h_t,
    T=sum_(t=1)^(n-1)h_t=S-ell,
    K_first_m=a^2/(1+2T).

Here K_first_m is the actual first-cut scalar payoff from accepted
`b98bb807d514e80d`. It is not a new benchmark. B_n is the ORIGINAL
Zhang--Jiang denominator, so the benchmark objective is 1/B_n.
The elementary bounds 6/25<a<1/2 give 1<ell<3/2.

Define the following restricted class of legal instances on this schedule.
Normalize smoothness to 1, choose a closest minimizer as 0, and normalize
the true initial distance to ||X_0||=1. Write g_i=grad F(X_i),
f_i=F(X_i)-F(0), with the actual GD displacement

    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i*g_i.

If a queried gradient is zero, the terminal gap is zero. Otherwise write
g_i=A_i*u_i, A_i>0, ||u_i||=1, for 0<=i<n. The sole queried-Gram
restriction is that there is a number tau in (0,1) with

    <u_0,u_i>=-tau                  (1<=i<n),
    <u_i,u_j>=tau                   (1<=i<j<n).          (1)

Every amplitude, initial anchor, sampled value, and actual function is
otherwise free. Tau may depend arbitrarily on m. In particular the
unqueried terminal gradient g_n is unrestricted. The Gram in (1) is
(1-tau)I+tau*eta*eta^T, where eta_0=1 and eta_i=-1 for i>=1.
It has no orthogonal queried pair and has a negative initial correlation
with every later query. Thus C89's positive-history premise fails and its
large orthogonal selection is absent.

Let V_best_m be the supremum of 2f_n over this class, including all dimensions.
The proposed joint conclusions are:

1. A fully explicit dense character construction, all original pair
   constraints, one static function, unique minimizer, and radius 1 are
   given below. It has an associative physical-tail boundary rule with
   all history and value costs retained.
2. For every m>=4, one specified construction has B_n*2f_n>1/150 and
   minimum character atom exactly 1/[8n(n+1)]. No scalar strictification
   or unpriced anchor component is required.
3. More strongly, the ENTIRE class (1) has the exact asymptotic strength

       lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)*V_best_m
          = Z_1/[2e*sigma^2] < 1/5,                    (2)

   where Z_1=lim sigma^(-m)B_(2^m-1) is the accepted ray constant and
   e=lim (1+1/m)^m. This includes arbitrary horizon-dependent tau and
   free terminal gradients, not merely the displayed construction.

The upper bound in (2) is about this family of lower witnesses. It is
NOT an upper bound on unrestricted W_n(h).

## 2. A direct construction on an arbitrary eligible physical word

First let n>=2, h_0=1+a with a>0, and choose 0<tau<1 such that

    h_i>=1/tau, 1<=i<n.                                (3)

Choose unit vectors u_0,...,u_n with exactly the signed Gram (1), now
including i=n with negative sign. Such vectors are realized explicitly
in Section 6. Choose q>0 and define

    A_0=1, A_i=q*tau^(i-1) for 1<=i<=n,
    g_i=A_i*u_i, X_0=g_0,
    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.

Define the actual tail moments

    P_i=sum_(t=1)^(i-1)h_t*A_t,
    E_i=sum_(t=i)^(n-1)h_t*A_t^2,  1<=i<=n.

Empty sums are zero. Let b>0 satisfy

    A_n+b/A_n+tau*P_n <= tau*a.                        (4)

Set f_0=1/2 and

    f_i=A_i^2/2+b+tau^2*E_i, 1<=i<=n,                 (5)

and include the optimum jet (0,0,0). These are values on original indices;
no trajectory is compressed or reset. The following sections verify EVERY
ordered interpolation residual

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.         (6)

The necessity and sufficiency of Q_ij<=0, with the explicit construction
of one function, are accepted in `1252b8823bb5a290`.

## 3. Every finite tail pair, with the original displacement

For 1<=i<j<=n put

    W_ij=sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t*A_t,
    D_ij=f_i-f_j=(A_i^2-A_j^2)/2
                           +tau^2*sum_(t=i)^(j-1)h_t*A_t^2.

The identity for D_ij follows from (5), not from a compressed update.
The actual signed inner products give the following exact allowed interval:

    L_ij <= D_ij <= U_ij,
    L_ij=tau*A_j*W_ij+(A_i^2+A_j^2-2tau*A_i*A_j)/2,
    U_ij=h_i*A_i^2+tau*A_i*sum_(t=i+1)^(j-1)h_t*A_t
                         -(A_i^2+A_j^2-2tau*A_i*A_j)/2. (7)

Here Q_ji=L_ij-D_ij and Q_ij=D_ij-U_ij. In particular every skipped
history term h_t*<g_i,g_t> is present in (7).

The lower inequality follows from the identity

    D_ij-L_ij
      =sum_(t=i)^(j-1)tau*h_t*A_t*(tau*A_t-A_j)
                          +A_j*(tau*A_i-A_j) >=0.      (8)

For each summand t<=j-1, tau*A_t>=A_j, because A_(t+1)=tau*A_t.
The last term is also nonnegative. For j=i+1 both terms vanish, giving
the exact reverse-row equality Q_(i+1,i)=0.

For the upper inequality put d=j-i. Direct expansion gives

    (U_ij-D_ij)/A_i^2
      =(h_i-1)(1-tau^2)
       +sum_(k=1)^(d-1)(h_(i+k)-1)*tau^(k+1)*(1-tau^(k+1))
       +J_d,

    J_d=-tau^2+tau^(d+1)
                   +sum_(k=1)^(d-1)(tau^(k+1)-tau^(2k+2)). (9)

One has J_1=0 and

    J_(d+1)-J_d=tau^(d+2)*(1-tau^d)>=0.

All coefficients in the first two terms of (9) are nonnegative because
h_i>=1. Thus U_ij-D_ij>=0. This proves both orientations for every
tail pair, not just adjacent samples.

## 4. Every optimum and initial pair; the exact scalar boundary

The negative initial history is retained exactly:

    <g_i,X_i>=tau*A_i*(a-P_i), 1<=i<=n.               (10)

The contribution of the first displacement is +h_0*tau*A_i, while
<g_i,X_0>=-tau*A_i. Deleting that signed initial term would change (10).

The optimum residual Q_(i,star) is nonpositive precisely when

    R_i:=A_i+b/A_i+tau*P_i+tau^2*E_i/A_i <= tau*a.

For 1<=i<n, expansion using A_(i+1)=tau*A_i gives

    R_(i+1)-R_i
      =(1-tau)[(tau*h_i-1)A_i
                    +tau*E_(i+1)/A_i+b/(tau*A_i)] >=0. (11)

Condition (3) is used here. Consequently every optimum upper row follows
from the SINGLE final boundary (4). Conversely the final row requires
(4), so this is an exact elimination for this chosen construction.
The other optimum orientation is

    Q_(star,i)=-b-tau^2*E_i<0.

At i=0, X_0=g_0 and f_0=1/2 give both optimum residuals zero.

For i>=1, direct calculation gives

    Q_(i,0)=Q_(i,star),
    Q_(0,i)=-a-b-tau^2*E_i+tau*(P_i+A_i).             (12)

If i<n, then P_n-P_i>=h_i*A_i>=A_i. Hence
tau*(P_i+A_i)<=tau*P_n<=tau*a by (4).
If i=n, (4) gives tau*P_n+A_n<=tau*a, so
tau*(P_n+A_n)<=tau*a-(1-tau)A_n<=tau*a.
In either case Q_(0,i)<=-(1-tau)a-b-tau^2*E_i<0.

Equations (7)--(12), together with the two zero rows at i=0, check ALL
original directed pairs including the terminal. For the smallest physical
block, indices 0,1 and star, they give Q_(1,0)=Q_(1,star)<=0 and
Q_(0,1)<0 with <g_0,g_1>=-tau*q<0. This block is part of the original
word with its actual h_0, and already has genuinely signed history.

## 5. One static function, values, and exact closest distance

Put y_i=X_i-g_i and d_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Then y_0=0,d_0=0 and
d_i=b+tau^2*E_i>0 for i>=1. Define on the same Euclidean space

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max{0,max_(1<=i<=n)(<y_i,v>-d_i)},
    F(X)=sup_v(<X,v>-H(v)).                            (13)

By the explicit all-pair interpolation construction in accepted
`1252b8823bb5a290`, F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and
1-smooth, and F(X_i)=f_i, grad F(X_i)=g_i for every i, including 0 and n.
Thus its actual GD is exactly the prescribed original trajectory.

All d_i with i>=1 are strictly positive, so H(v)=||v||^2/2 in a
neighborhood of zero. Also H(v)>=||v||^2/2 everywhere. It follows that
F(0)=0. For any X!=0, take a sufficiently small t in (0,1) with tX in
that neighborhood. Then

    F(X)>=(t-t^2/2)||X||^2>0.

Therefore argmin F={0}. Its true initial squared distance is exactly
||X_0||^2=||g_0||^2=1. No inverse-Gram or orthogonal-anchor radius cost is
hidden. Multiplication of F by L and use of physical steps h_i/L give
the same normalized payoff for arbitrary L>0.

## 6. A positive finite character law

Let d be any integer with 2^d>=n+1. In the (d+1)-bit label space use

    lambda_0=(0,binary(0)),
    lambda_i=(1,binary(i)), 1<=i<=n.

These labels do not permute the physical indices. Let s range over this
binary space, let e_sig have only its first bit equal to 1, and put

    D=2^(d+1),
    w_s=tau*1_(s=e_sig)+(1-tau)/D,
    (u_i)_s=sqrt(w_s)*(-1)^(s dot lambda_i).            (14)

All weights are positive and sum to one. Summing the binary characters
by pairing a differing bit gives

    <u_i,u_j>=tau*eta_i*eta_j+(1-tau)*1_(i=j).

This is the required full signed Gram, not a PSD-only existence claim:
positions, all values, all original residuals and F were supplied above.
The explicit anchor is simply X_0=u_0. On H_m one may take d=m,D=2N.
The least atom is (1-tau)/(2N).

## 7. Exact payoff and an associative physical block rule

Put

    r=tau^(n-1), H_tail=sum_(t=1)^(n-1)h_t*tau^(t-1).

Then A_n=q*r and P_n=q*H_tail. For fixed q, the largest permitted b in
(4) is

    b=q*r*(tau*a-q*(tau*H_tail+r)).                   (15)

When b>0, (13) has the unique minimizer just proved. Its normalized
terminal payoff is

    V=2f_n=2q*r*tau*a-q^2*r*(2tau*H_tail+r).

Completing the square in q gives the maximum, at

    q=tau*a/(2tau*H_tail+r),
    b=q^2*r*tau*H_tail>0,
    V_tau=tau^2*a^2*r/(2tau*H_tail+r).                 (16)

This is an algebraic formula, not a numerical parameter evaluation.

For a consecutive tail block p=(p_0,...,p_(k-1)), define

    alpha_p=tau^k,
    H_p=sum_(j=0)^(k-1)p_j*tau^j,
    J_p=sum_(j=0)^(k-1)p_j*tau^(2j).

If it starts at tail amplitude A, cumulative moment P, and value f, its
actual end state is

    A_out=alpha_p*A,
    P_out=P+A*H_p,
    f_out=f-A^2*(1-alpha_p^2)/2-tau^2*A^2*J_p.         (17)

For the physical concatenation p followed by qword,

    alpha_(p qword)=alpha_p*alpha_qword,
    H_(p qword)=H_p+alpha_p*H_qword,
    J_(p qword)=J_p+alpha_p^2*J_qword.                 (18)

These identities follow by splitting the defining sums at the actual
index k. They prove associativity without resetting P, A, or the enclosing
schedule parameters. All cross-block Q rows are already covered by
(7)--(12). The final boundary (4) prices the complete history. This is a
legal iterable rule for this restricted tail geometry, not an improving
primitive-capacity composition theorem.

There is an explicit reserve test when appending to a fixed existing
prefix. Write d_in=f-A^2/2. The inherited values remain unchanged, while

    d_out=d_in-tau^2*A^2*J_p.

A permitted append must have d_out>0 and

    A_out+d_out/A_out+tau*P_out<=tau*a.

With these tests, the enlarged transcript has the same form (5), with
b=d_out: splitting its future sum shows that every old value is exactly
its previous value. Thus the all-pair proof applies to the old and new
samples together. If d_out<0 the original optimum lower row fails; if
the displayed boundary fails, the original optimum upper row fails.
No claim is made that an arbitrary further block passes these paid tests.

Since tau*H_tail>=r*T, (16) also gives

    V_tau<=tau^2*a^2/(2T+1)<K_first_m.                       (19)

Thus even before the wider obstruction below, the exact block rule does
not improve the scalar first-cut lower bound.

## 8. Quantitative calibration on every original H_m

Choose tau=1-1/(4n). For n>=15,
1/tau<=60/59<31/25<ell, so (3) holds at every original tail step.
The character law's minimum atom is exactly 1/[8nN]. Since H_tail<=T
and r<=1, (16) and Bernoulli's inequality yield

    V_tau >= tau^(n+1)*K_first_m
           >=[1-(n+1)/(4n)]*K_first_m >=(11/15)*K_first_m.        (20)

For completeness the original benchmark comparison uses its exact mass
recurrences, not the physical mass as a substitute. The accepted joins are

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1)Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1)G(C_k,B_l).

Both joins are increasing and G>=Omega: after squaring the nonnegative
quantities 3u+sqrt(v^2+8uv) and sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2), their squared difference
is 8u^2+2uv+6u*sqrt(v^2+8uv)>=0. Induction gives B_n>=C_n, and the
balanced primitive split gives B_(2^m-1)>=sigma^m.

Counting 2^(m-k-1) occurrences of level k in the actual word gives

    S=N-1+r_sum*(c^m-N),
    r_sum=delta/[c(c-2)], 0<r_sum<1.                  (21)

Here c>sigma>2, and c(c-2)>sigma(sigma-2)=1>delta. Hence
S<c^m-1 and 2T+1<2c^m<6sigma^m. The last inequality uses
(1+1/m)^m<3, obtained by the binomial expansion and sum 1/k!<3.
Finally a=(sqrt(2)-1)(m-1)/(m+1)>6/25 for m>=4. Therefore

    B_n*V_tau >(11/15)*a^2/6
              >396/56250 >1/150.                    (22)

This proves a fully realized, quantitatively priced signed construction
on every original enclosing horizon, while (19) records its limitation.

## 9. Arbitrary amplitudes: an original tail-pair restriction

We now return to the ENTIRE class (1), with arbitrary amplitudes, values,
anchor, and free terminal. Set k=n-1, so the last queried sample is k.
Normalize using the true closest-minimizer distance as in Section 1.

For 1<=i<j<=k, add Q_ij and Q_ji. The accepted necessary inequality is

    <g_i-g_j,X_i-X_j> >= ||g_i-g_j||^2.

Its LEFT side is exactly

    h_i*A_i*(A_i-tau*A_j)
      +tau*(A_i-A_j)*sum_(t=i+1)^(j-1)h_t*A_t.        (23)

If A_j>=A_i/tau, both terms in (23) are nonpositive. The right side is
A_i^2+A_j^2-2tau*A_i*A_j>0 because 0<tau<1 and both amplitudes are
positive. This contradiction proves

    A_i>tau*A_j, 1<=i<j<=k.                           (24)

No nonnegative-history simplification was made across the initial sample.
The assertion is only within the tail where its pair correlations are
actually positive.

## 10. The true anchor and a free terminal: a sharp limiting upper bound

Write A_0=||g_0||. Optimum cocoercivity gives
<X_0,g_0>>=A_0^2, and A_0<=||X_0||=1. Set z=<X_0,u_0>, so z>=A_0.
Resolve the unit vector u_k=-tau*u_0+sqrt(1-tau^2)*v with v perpendicular
to u_0 and ||v||=1. Then

    <X_0,u_k> <= -tau*z+sqrt(1-tau^2)*sqrt(1-z^2).

The right side is decreasing for 0<=z<=1, so it is at most its value at
z=A_0. Put T_pre=sum_(t=1)^(k-1)h_t=T-ell. The original optimum row at k,
including the negative contribution of g_0 and every positive tail term,
therefore gives, using (24),

    f_k <= A_k[tau*a*A_0+sqrt(1-tau^2)*sqrt(1-A_0^2)]
                               -(tau^2*T_pre+1/2)A_k^2.

Cauchy--Schwarz bounds the bracket by

    M_tau=sqrt(tau^2*a^2+1-tau^2).

The actual final step has length ell<2. Smoothness at X_k gives

    f_n<=f_k-ell*(1-ell/2)*A_k^2.

Maximizing the resulting quadratic in A_k proves the uniform class bound

    2f_n <= U_angle(tau)
      :=[tau^2*a^2+1-tau^2]
                    /[1+2tau^2*T_pre+ell*(2-ell)].    (25)

This does not impose any law on g_n. Its initial norm was the TRUE radius,
and neither a translated reset nor an unrecorded anchor adjustment occurs.
Relative to the first-cut payoff, (25) implies

    U_angle(tau)/K_first_m
      <=[1+(tau^(-2)-1)/a^2]
                     *(1+2T)/(1+2T-ell^2).           (26)

In particular, as tau tends to 1 this ratio tends to 1 uniformly over m
because a>6/25, ell<3/2 and T>=n-1.

## 11. Small correlations: a priced moment bound on the actual tail

There are M=n-1 tail queries 1,...,k. For consecutive tail samples, the
original reverse interpolation inequality reads

    f_i-f_(i+1) >= (A_i^2+A_(i+1)^2)/2
                          +tau*(h_i-1)*A_i*A_(i+1)
                    >= (A_i^2+A_(i+1)^2)/2.           (27)

At each tail sample, the optimum row and the actual displacement from X_1
give

    <X_1,g_i> >= f_i+A_i^2/2.

All added terms in this last relation are tau*h_t*A_i*A_t>=0 for
1<=t<i. Write b_tail=f_k-A_k^2/2>=0. Summing (27) gives

    <X_1,u_i> >= [b_tail+sum_(j=i)^k A_j^2]/A_i >0.   (28)

For real coefficients t_i, (1) gives the elementary Gram estimate

    ||sum_(i=1)^k t_i*u_i||^2
       =(1-tau)sum_i t_i^2+tau*(sum_i t_i)^2
       <=[1+(M-1)tau]*sum_i t_i^2.

Apply Cauchy--Schwarz with t_i=<X_1,u_i>. It follows that

    sum_i <X_1,u_i>^2 <=[1+(M-1)tau]||X_1||^2.        (29)

The original first step costs no hidden radius: since ell<2 and
<X_0,g_0>>=A_0^2,

    ||X_1||^2<=1-ell*(2-ell)*A_0^2<=1.

The scalar elimination proved in accepted `0e7e78056dc6258c`, proof
Section 5, applies to (28)--(29): if q_i=A_i^2 and T_i=b_tail+sum_(j>=i)q_j,
it bounds 2f_k by beta_(M-1) times the upper budget for sum T_i^2/q_i.
Here beta_j=4/[1+sqrt(1+2c_j)]^2, c_0=0,
c_(j+1)=c_j+2+2sqrt(c_j+1), with beta_j<=2/j^2 for j>=1.
The actual last step cannot increase f. Hence

    2f_n <= [1+(n-2)tau]*beta_(n-2)
           <= 2[1+(n-2)tau]/(n-2)^2.                 (30)

This is an elementary extension with its full Gram cost written out.
No orthogonality of these actual tail gradients is asserted.

## 12. Intermediate correlations: exact adjacent ratios

For two consecutive tail queries the same cocoercivity inequality gives

    (h_i-1)A_i^2-A_(i+1)^2
                    +(2-h_i)tau*A_i*A_(i+1)>=0.

For z>0 define the positive root

    r_tau(z)=[-tau*(z-1)+sqrt(tau^2*(z-1)^2+4z)]/2.

Then A_(i+1)/A_i<=r_tau(h_i-1). The root obeys r_tau(z)>tau and is
strictly increasing in z. A convenient exact formula, with natural log,
is

    phi_tau(x):=log r_tau(exp x)
       =x/2-asinh(tau*sinh(x/2)).                     (31)

Indeed divide r^2+tau(z-1)r-z=0 by r*sqrt(z) and solve the resulting
hyperbolic-sine identity. Thus phi is odd, phi(0)=0, and

    phi'(x)=1/2-tau*cosh(x/2)
                              /[2sqrt(1+tau^2*sinh^2(x/2))]>0,
    phi''(x)=-tau*(1-tau^2)*sinh(x/2)
                              /[4(1+tau^2*sinh^2(x/2))^(3/2)]. (32)

The derivative positivity follows by squaring: the denominator under its
square root exceeds tau^2*cosh^2(x/2) by 1-tau^2. On x>0, phi is
strictly concave. These formulas also prove r_tau(z)>tau by its limit
as z decreases to zero and strict monotonicity.

The first queried norm is bounded without assuming it shares a positive
correlation. Write d=g_1-g_0. Cocoercivity and X_1-X_0=-ell*g_0 give
-ell*<g_0,d>>=||d||^2, whence

    ||g_1||^2<=||g_0||^2+(1-2/ell)||d||^2<=||g_0||^2<=1.

Define the product over ALL original steps

    P_all=product_(t=0)^(n-1) r_tau(h_t-1).

The first and last physical steps both have excess a. Multiplying only
the legitimate tail inequalities i=1,...,n-2 therefore gives

    A_k <= P_all/r_tau(a)^2 <= tau^(-2)*P_all.         (33)

This product does not assume any condition on the free terminal gradient.

## 13. Ruler counts create a uniform strict product loss

Put Lc=log c. Since the physical excess at level j is delta*c^(j-1)
with delta<1, monotonicity in (31) and the original counts give

    log P_all
      <=(N/2)[-phi(Lc)+sum_(j=2)^(m-1)2^(-j)*phi((j-1)Lc)].

Concavity and phi(0)=0 imply phi(r*Lc)<=r*phi(Lc) for integer r>=1.
Keep the strict deficit at j=3 separately and write

    Delta_tau(c)=2phi(Lc)-phi(2Lc)>0.

The finite identity sum_(j=2)^(m-1)(j-1)/2^j=1-m/2^(m-1) yields

    log P_all <= -m*phi(Lc)-(N/16)*Delta_tau(c).        (34)

This retains all original levels and their actual multiplicities.

For an explicit bound, c>=sigma>2 and c<4 for m>=4. Hence
Lc>1/2 and cosh(Lc)<5/2. The identity

    Delta_tau(c)=integral_(t=0)^Lc integral_(s=t)^(t+Lc)
                                           [-phi''(s)] ds dt

contains the rectangle Lc/2<=t<=Lc, Lc<=s<=3Lc/2. On this rectangle,
sinh(s/2)>1/4, and
(1+tau^2*sinh^2(s/2))^(3/2)<=cosh(Lc)^3<125/8.
Its area is Lc^2/4>1/16. Substitution into (32) proves

    Delta_tau(c)>=tau*(1-tau^2)/4000
                         >=tau*(1-tau)/4000.          (35)

For clarity Lc>1/2 follows from log 2=integral_1^2 dt/t>1/2;
the other bounds are direct inequalities, not numerical evaluation.
Combining (33)--(35), and dropping the nonpositive -m*phi(Lc), gives

    A_k <=tau^(-2)*exp[-N*tau*(1-tau)/64000].

Finally retain the signed original displacement in the optimum row:

    f_k<=<g_k,X_k>
       =<g_k,X_0>+ell*tau*A_0*A_k
                    -tau*A_k*sum_(t=1)^(k-1)h_t*A_t
       <=(1+ell*tau)*A_k <=(5/2)*A_k.

The final physical step is again nonincreasing for f. Thus the entire
class with free terminal satisfies

    2f_n <=5tau^(-2)*exp[-N*tau*(1-tau)/64000].         (36)

This closes fixed correlations bounded away from 0 and 1 exponentially.
It does not discard the regime tau tending to either endpoint.

## 14. Uniform control of every horizon-dependent correlation

Accepted `04219554e41d9750` gives

    sigma^(-m)*B_(2^m-1) -> Z_1 in (0,infinity).

Hence B_(2^m-1)=O(N^rho), rho=log_2(sigma)<3/2.
Split ALL tau in (0,1) into three exhaustive ranges.

* If tau<=N^(-1/2), (30) is at most
  2(1+sqrt(N))/(N-3)^2. Multiplied by B_n, this tends to zero.
* If N^(-1/2)<=tau<=1-N^(-1/2), then
  tau*(1-tau)>=(1/2)N^(-1/2), and tau^(-2)<=N.
  Equation (36) is at most 5N*exp[-sqrt(N)/128000].
  Its product with B_n also tends to zero.
* If tau>=1-N^(-1/2), (26), a>6/25, ell<3/2 and T>=n-1 give

       2f_n <=[1+o(1)]*K_first_m,                           (37)

  where the o(1) is uniform over this full range. Explicitly its
  multiplicative factor is bounded by

       [1+((1-N^(-1/2))^(-2)-1)/(6/25)^2]
                       *(1+2T)/(1+2T-9/4),

  which tends to 1.

Consequently the supremum over the entire class has

    limsup B_n*V_best_m <= lim B_n*K_first_m,                     (38)

once the limit on the right is evaluated below. No interchange of a
nonuniform pointwise limit with the supremum was made; the three estimates
are uniform over their respective ranges.

## 15. Attainment of the limiting class strength and its original-B value

For the explicit construction choose instead tau_m=1-1/n^2. Condition
(3) holds on every H_m, since 1/tau_m<=225/224<31/25<ell. Moreover
tau_m^(n+1)->1, r=tau_m^(n-1)->1, and (16),(19),(20)'s first inequality
give the squeeze

    tau_m^(n+1)*K_first_m <= V_(tau_m) <= K_first_m.

Thus V_(tau_m)/K_first_m tends to 1. This construction is in the queried class
(1) and supplies a legal terminal, so V_best_m>=V_(tau_m).

In (21), r_sum tends to 1, (2/sigma)^m tends to zero, and
c^m/sigma^m=(1+1/m)^m tends to e. Therefore

    T/sigma^m -> e,  a -> 1/sigma,
    B_n*K_first_m -> Z_1/[2e*sigma^2].                      (39)

Equations (38)--(39) and the construction squeeze prove (2).

The strict numerical comparison in (2) needs no computation. In the
accepted ray enclosure, use b=1,j=0. Its UPPER endpoint involves B_1
(not B_0), so B_1=G(1,1)=4 gives

    Z_1 <= (sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2 = 41209/10000.

Since e>2 and sigma^2=3+2sqrt(2)>29/5,

    Z_1/[2e*sigma^2]
       < (41209/10000)/(116/5)
       =206045/1160000 <1/5.

The ray theorem is used only in its unconditional scalar part. No
four-step seed existence is assumed anywhere in this argument.

## 16. Scope, an actual uncovered freedom, and failed extrapolations

This result closes the one-initial-sign CONSTANT-CORRELATION class (1),
including arbitrary amplitudes, values, initial anchors, horizon-dependent
tau, and a free terminal. It includes the queried geometry of the C91
dense lift but does not use C91 as a proof premise. It does not close
general signed character laws, nonconstant magnitudes of correlations,
more than one chronological sign reversal, or the changed affine-child
geometry pursued by max.

Multiple sign reversals are an actual legal freedom on the SAME original
schedule. For example put

    kappa=(1+1/ell)/2, F_quad(x)=kappa*x^2/2, x_0=1.

Then 0<kappa<1, kappa*h_0=(ell+1)/2>1, and h_1>ell gives kappa*h_1>1.
Thus the first three nonzero gradients have signs +,-,+, outside (1).
For the complete original word its exact data are

    x_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa*h_t),
    g_i=kappa*x_i, f_i=kappa*x_i^2/2,
    Q_ij=-kappa*(1-kappa)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0

including the optimum. Its single static function has unique minimizer 0,
true radius 1, and actual payoff
kappa*product_(t<n)(1-kappa*h_t)^2. This is an explicit scope check only;
no favorable asymptotic payoff is claimed. In (23), its intervening
signed contributions would not have the two nonpositive signs used there.

The next missing object is a quantitative multiple-sign or nonconstant-
correlation boundary rule, or another legal geometry improving the
underlying trajectory, with every original cross pair and true radius.
A covariance matrix, a relabeling without value constraints, or optimizing
tau inside (1) cannot provide that improvement. The current exact block
rule is iterable but has the completely priced ceiling (2).

For coordination, the construction's full interface is: the signed Gram
(14), anchor X_0=g_0, values (5), actual positions in Section 2, boundary
(4), state update (17), and all cross-pair identities (7)--(12). It has
active reverse tail pairs and both optimum rows active at sample 0; it
does not have C91's strict margin on every distinct pair. No insertion
into a different child word is licensed without matching these data.

All statements in this manuscript remain a joint author proof draft.
No verifier acceptance, original-target answer, resource renewal, or extra
runtime round is inferred.

# C59: a fully specified legal H6 adversary above 4/M6

Status: **exact finite reconstruction and joint proof candidate; UNVERIFIED**.
No new fact is claimed and no verifier submission is allocated. The only
accepted mathematical predecessor is `1252b8823bb5a290`, for full
smooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate construction.

This is a finite diagnosis of the actual H6 word, not an asymptotic theorem.
The word here uses epsilon=1/6. It is not the length-63 subword obtained by
fixing epsilon=1/m for a much larger horizon m.

## 1. Statement and exact input object

Put

    c=(7/6)(1+sqrt(2)), a=5(sqrt(2)-1)/7,
    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<6,
    h=H6, n=63, S=sum_(i<63)h_i, M6=1+S.

There is a completely specified convex differentiable 1-smooth function
on R^66 with unique minimizer0 and an actual GD trajectory for this h
such that

    2(F(X_63)-F(0))/dist(X_0,argmin F)^2
             > 56/2711 > 1/50 > 4/M6.              (1)

In particular, this finite word cannot have a full valid upper certificate
at level1/M6. No assertion is made about eventual large-m feasibility.

The rational construction data are the lower-triangular integer matrix T
of order65 and the64 integer values v_i in

    research/max3_c59_h6_rational_primal.json.

The PUBLIC file's SHA256 is

    7376e26ca81edc6438c0563e008c845ce439e424bd42f9f65c37a07867d6b113.

Its fields `coordinate_integer_rows_lower_triangular` and
`function_value_numerators` are part of the definition, with the common
denominator D=10^15. For reproducibility the complete defining integer
data are also appended to the shared proof finding. No numerical solver
claim is used as a premise of the construction below.

Let the rational vectors in R^65 be

    v_start=T[0,:]/D,  q_i=T[i+1,:]/D (0<=i<=63),
    u_i=v_i/D.

Define the core positions by the EXACT algebraic GD recursion

    z_0=v_start,
    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i q_i.                            (2)

Here q_63 is not used in (2), but will be the core terminal gradient.
The original numerical Gram is not treated as an exact Gram. These
rational vectors define their own positive semidefinite Gram by dot
products, regardless of any properties of the source solver output.

## 2. The finite exact checks, with their integer certificate

For these core data include the zero optimum jet. Define

    Q^core_ij=u_i-u_j+<q_i,z_j-z_i>+||q_i-q_j||^2/2,
    delta^core_i=u_i-||q_i||^2/2,

with the star's position, gradient and value all zero. The following
strict bounds are verified by integer comparisons of the defining data:

    Q^core_ij < eta=1/500000000000 for EVERY i!=j,
    delta^core_i > -eta for every finite i,
    ||v_start||^2 < 1001/1000,
    u_63 > 21/2000,
    200<S<256,  h_i>1 for every i.                  (3)

The first line checks all65*64=4160 ordered pairs, including both optimum
directions and both terminal directions. Here are the exact formulas used
to audit (3), so the finite part is not a feasibility-tolerance assertion.

The data file gives each step as

    h_t=(A_t+B_t sqrt(2))/54432

with integer arrays A_t,B_t. These are exactly the recursively defined
steps: arithmetic in Q(sqrt(2)) with a=(-5/7,5/7) and c=(7/6,7/6)
produces those integer pairs. Let

    G_ab=sum_k T[a,k]T[b,k].

Thus <q_i,q_j>=G_(i+1,j+1)/D^2 and
<q_i,v_start>=G_(i+1,0)/D^2. Set Hden=54432 and define integer arrays

    R_ij=Hden G_(i+1,0)-sum_(t<j) A_t G_(i+1,t+1),
    I_ij=                  -sum_(t<j) B_t G_(i+1,t+1).

Then <q_i,z_j>=(R_ij+I_ij sqrt(2))/(Hden D^2).
When j=star, set both of these numerators to zero. When i=star, every
term containing q_i is zero. Set v_star=0 and the corresponding gradient
Gram entries to zero. The exact pair numerator formula is

    Qa_ij=2 Hden D(v_i-v_j)+2(R_ij-R_ii)
                   +Hden(G_(i+1,i+1)+G_(j+1,j+1)-2G_(i+1,j+1)),
    Qb_ij=2(I_ij-I_ii),
    Q^core_ij=(Qa_ij+Qb_ij sqrt(2))/(2 Hden D^2),    (4)

using the displayed star conventions. The missing diagonal terms for a
star index in (4) are zero.

For the radical, use the rational interval

    Lrad/10^50 < sqrt(2) < (Lrad+1)/10^50,
    Lrad=141421356237309504880168872420969807856967187537694.

Its validity follows by squaring the two positive integer endpoints.
For each pair an upper-bound numerator with denominator
2 Hden D^2*10^50 is

    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*(Lrad+1)  if Qb_ij>=0,
    Qa_ij*10^50+Qb_ij*Lrad      if Qb_ij<0.          (5)

The data file contains all4160 integer triples from (4)--(5). Every one
satisfies `upper_numerator*500000000000 < denominator`. The largest
upper bound occurs at the directed pair (31,30) and is approximately
1.371014491112536e-12; the exact numerator and denominator are retained
in `audit.max_Q_upper_numerator` and `audit.max_Q_upper_denominator`.
This decimal is descriptive, whereas the integer comparison proves the
inequality used in (3).

The other checks in (3) use the same radical interval for S and each h,
and use, without a radical,

    delta^core_i=(2v_i D-G_(i+1,i+1))/(2D^2),
    ||v_start||^2=G_00/D^2.

The minimum core delta is already positive in these data (approximately
8.5986e-11), but only the weaker stated lower bound is needed. The audit
script is PUBLIC at `research/max3_c59_h6_exact_primal_repair.py`.
Its single finite integer check completed in under0.1second and used no
optimization, search, eigenvalue clipping, or large symbolic computation.
This is a transparent finite arithmetic component of an unverified proof
candidate; it is not a substitute for the verifier's acceptance.

## 3. One controlled auxiliary coordinate repairs all pairs

Choose

    kappa=1/512, t0=1/8,
    xi_0=1, xi_(i+1)=(1-kappa h_i)xi_i.

Every factor is positive since h_i<=S<256. For any initial segment,
the elementary product inequality

    product_t(1-a_t)>=1-sum_t a_t  (a_t>=0, sum a_t<1)

follows inductively from (1-a)(1-b)>=1-a-b. It gives

    1>=xi_i>=1-kappa S>1/2.

For i<j, positivity and h_i>1 imply

    xi_i-xi_j>=xi_i-xi_(i+1)=kappa h_i xi_i>kappa/2.

The distance to the optimum sample xi_star=0 is also greater than
kappa/2. Thus these bounds cover every distinct pair, including star.

Define the66-dimensional jets and values

    X_i=(z_i,t0 xi_i),
    G_i=(q_i,t0 kappa xi_i),
    f_i=u_i+t0^2 kappa xi_i^2/2
       =u_i+xi_i^2/65536, 0<=i<=63,
    X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0.                      (6)

Their GD recursion is EXACT by (2) and the recursion for xi. The full
interpolation residual separates by coordinates into

    Q_ij=Q^core_ij
          -t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)(xi_i-xi_j)^2/2.      (7)

This is a polynomial identity, not a claimed separability of the final
function. The second term is the residual for the single quadratic
auxiliary coordinate at the displayed samples. Since1-kappa>1/2,
its magnitude is strictly greater than

    t0^2 kappa^3/16=2^(-37)>eta.

Combining with (3) proves Q_ij<0 for ALL4160 ordered pairs simultaneously.
The same common values and coordinates are used in every pair.

## 4. One global function and the true initial distance

For each finite sample put

    Y_i=X_i-G_i, delta_i=f_i-||G_i||^2/2.

Equation (6) gives

    delta_i=delta^core_i+t0^2 kappa(1-kappa)xi_i^2/2
             >-eta+t0^2 kappa/16>0.                (8)

Add the star with Y_star=0,delta_star=0, and define

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in {star,0,...,63})
                              [<Y_i,v>-delta_i],
    F(X)=sup_v[<X,v>-H(v)].                         (9)

By accepted1252b8823bb5a290 and (7), this is one globally defined finite
convex differentiable function with1-Lipschitz gradient, interpolating
every value and gradient in (6). Therefore its GD trajectory is the one
already specified, with the actual ordered word H6.

The finite set of strict gaps in (8) makes the star's zero affine function
uniquely active in a neighborhood of v=0. Hence H(v)=||v||^2/2 there and
partial H(0)={0}. The maximizing-vector condition for (9) identifies the
minimizers of F with partial H(0), as in the accepted predecessor. Thus
argmin F={0}; no remote or nonclosest minimizer is used in normalization.

The true squared distance and terminal value satisfy

    R^2=||v_start||^2+1/64 < 1001/1000+1/64
                              =8133/8000,
    2F(X_63)=2u_63+t0^2 kappa xi_63^2 >21/1000.

Consequently

    2F(X_63)/R^2> (21/1000)/(8133/8000)
                 =56/2711>1/50>4/(1+S),

which proves (1), subject only to the explicitly supplied finite integer
checks on the defining data. If a unit initial norm is desired, use
F_unit(x)=F(Rx)/R^2 and initial point X_0/R; the smoothness remains1,
the steps remain h, and the normalized payoff is unchanged. Multiplication
of the function by any L>0 supplies the corresponding L-smooth instance
with steps h_i/L.

## 5. A concrete feature of the surviving geometry

The rational core gradients have the exact inner product

    <q_35,q_36>
      =-14715168270572693173715883215/10^30
      <-14/1000.

The extra coordinate changes this by at most t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24),
since0<xi_i<=1. Thus

    <G_35,G_36><-1/100.

Both indices are queried. The actual object retains substantial signed
gradient correlation; it is not a Huber common-gradient line, a mutually
orthogonal gradient transcript, or an all-gradients acute-cone trajectory.
The auxiliary coordinate repairs small interpolation errors in an already
strong correlated core, in contrast to attempting to repair the grossly
illegal fixed scalar C55 template. No claim is made that this description
is a scalable generating rule.

There is also a basis-independent distinction from a bounded-jump
coordinate construction. Let d_i=G_i-G_(i-1),1<=i<=63, and let J be their
Gram matrix. If an orthonormal coordinate system makes every coordinate
of the gradient list change at most k times, then

    2 sum_(i<j)|J_ij| <= (k-1) trace(J).             (10)

Indeed each scalar coordinate of the jump list has at most k nonzero
entries. Cauchy--Schwarz gives (sum|a_i|)^2<=k sum a_i^2, hence
2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|<=(k-1)sum a_i^2. Sum over coordinates and apply the
triangle inequality to the resulting off-diagonal entries. Since J is
unchanged by orthogonal changes of physical coordinates, (10) applies to
every such representation.

For the exact rational core, the integer increment Gram is saved PUBLICLY
in `research/max3_c59_exact_increment_gram.json`. Its entries follow
directly from the rational vectors already defined, with denominator D^2.
Exact integer summation gives

    trace(J_core) approximately 0.44249400386221016,
    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij| approximately 2.306374594722243,
    2sum_(i<j)|J_core_ij|-5trace(J_core)>9/100.       (11)

Only the last, rational inequality is used. The auxiliary jump vector is
a_i=t0 kappa(xi_i-xi_(i-1)); all entries are negative and
(sum|a_i|)^2<t0^2 kappa^2=2^(-24). Its contribution is aa^T. By the
triangle inequality, adding aa^T can lower the left side of (11) by at
most

    2sum_(i<j)|a_i a_j|+5sum_i a_i^2
           <=5(sum_i|a_i|)^2<5*2^(-24).

Thus the full repaired J still satisfies
2sum_(i<j)|J_ij|>5trace(J). Equation (10) rules out every representation
in which all coordinate gradients have at most SIX chronological jumps.
In particular this actual finite adversary is outside the common two-jump
primal cone. The large core margin in (11) ensures this distinction is
not caused only by the tiny auxiliary quadratic coordinate.

## 6. Scope and next open interface

This candidate is finite, exact in its definitions, and includes all
function values, gradients, positions, actual GD equations, and the true
distance. It does not rely on a numerically feasible Gram or a nearly
positive semidefinite dual. The source solver arrays are preserved
unchanged; the rational data and added coordinate are separately labeled
new objects.

The estimate rules out1/M6 for this one actual word. It does NOT refute an
upper bound valid only at sufficiently large horizons, establish a common
pattern for the changing parameters epsilon=1/m, or prove the all-schedule
Zhang--Jiang profile. In particular the length63 block changes physically
as the enclosing horizon changes. The missing scalable interface is an
analytic rule for the correlated core that controls ALL interpolation
pairs and distance under those parameter changes. Repeating this finite
data set, extrapolating its numerical eigenvectors, or fixing only one
turning pair would not provide that rule.

No routine verification allowance was used. This remains a complete
finite proof candidate, not an accepted fact.

# Separated caps: last-crossing capture and all smaller-tail terminals

**JOINT UNVERIFIED. No formal call, accepted fact, or minimax resolution.**

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; invocation87 after ended86;
completed ceiling87 / attempted88; one-invocation cap1. Operative guidance
44905a26e2e8dbff; plan32e39de52ebd2361. This is the authorized two-mechanism
analytical round. No automatic renewal is inferred.

The complete prior report37660b4102b12fe7, 42223 bytes, SHA256
1c02183c1f5f9408c0ad16049e7662f584901b5d0b9340e8475783ecc171cca4,
was read for awareness. Neither its balanced theorem nor its isolated
root all-tail state is treated as an accepted premise. The new results
below are derived together and are self-contained. TASK forbids
fact_submit, so none of these arguments has verifier acceptance.

## 1. Fixed family and the two candidate conclusions

Fix one integer m>=1536. At every depth keep exactly

    epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, b=c-2,
    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, D=1+2S_m,
    n=2^m-1.

The recursion uses 0<=j<m; d_m is an auxiliary scale and is not an
extra physical step. The hierarchy symbols here are LOCAL to this
report, consistent with the preceding report; they do not redefine
unrelated project-glossary uses of H_j, a, b, c, or A_j.

Fix 0<kappa<=1, v=1-kappa, and independent caps

    kappa<=K=Bsmall<B=Blarge, K/B<b.

Choose a fixed sign sigma_L in {+1,-1} for the larger-cap ray and
write sigma_S=-sigma_L for the smaller-cap ray. Put

    B_(sigma_L)=B, B_(sigma_S)=K,
    u_sigma=v+B_sigma,
    G_sigma(r)=min(B_sigma,max(kappa*r,r-v)), r>=0,
    g(sigma*r)=sigma*G_sigma(r), g(0)=0,
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt, F_sigma(r)=f(sigma*r).

There is one common radius-one core with curvature kappa on both
rays, one fixed outward curvature change, and two different caps.
The function never changes between children. For any signed start s,
Psi_j(s) is the actual output after the COMPLETE H_j on this function.
For the full word set x_0=s, x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g(x_i), where h_i are
the chronological entries of H_m, and R=|s|>0. The minimizer is0.
The plain R here denotes original radius, not the project's R_n.

A strict crossing means two consecutive nonzero iterates have opposite
signs. A zero hit stays zero forever.

**Candidate conclusion 1: the frozen asymmetric capture law.**
For every 1<=j<=m and every actual start, if the complete H_j has
at least one strict crossing and its nonzero terminal lies on the
larger-cap ray, then

    |g(Psi_j(s))| < b*K.                              (1.1)

More precisely, if the LAST strict crossing has physical level k
and remaining physical suffix mass T, then

    |g(Psi_j(s))| <= d_k*K/(1+T)
                   <= d_k*K/A_k < b*K.                (1.2)

This proves the requested non-strict bound with the frozen coefficient
b=c-2. On the smaller ray |g|<=K always; this does not mean that
the terminal is before its cap. No geometric amplitude grid is needed.

**Candidate conclusion 2: every smaller-tail terminal is paid.**
For every permitted function and original nonzero start, if

    x_n=sigma_S*r_n, r_n>=u_(sigma_S)=v+K,

then

    2f(x_n)/R^2 <= 1/D.                              (1.3)

Thus the requested form (1+C0/m)/D holds with the absolute C0=0
and explicit threshold m>=1536. More specifically:

* If this history has no strict crossing, its score is at most1/D.
* If it has any strict crossing, it has EXACTLY ONE, from the
  original larger ray into the smaller ray, and its score is
  strictly less than2/(3D).

The last assertion covers arbitrary source positions before that
crossing, arbitrary physical crossing levels, and all literal
prefixes and suffixes. It does not assume that the larger-ray
prefix is on its tail, or that the crossing occurs at the root.

The whole separated-cap family is NOT covered by (1.3): crossed
histories terminating on the larger ray, or strictly before the
smaller cap knot, still need a full original-radius objective bound.
This report also supplies no upper bound on the unrestricted
finite-dimensional supremum W_n(H_m). The original minimax target
is OPEN; W_n=2R_n is unchanged.

## 2. Actual objective, exact energies, and physical transcript

For each ray separately, the derivative is

    G_sigma(r)=kappa*r,              0<=r<=1,
               r-v,                 1<=r<=u_sigma,
               B_sigma,             r>=u_sigma.

The middle may collapse when B_sigma=kappa. Direct integration gives

    2F_sigma(r)=kappa*r^2,                        0<=r<=1,
                (r-v)^2+kappa*v,                 1<=r<=u_sigma,
                2B_sigma*r-Ctail_sigma,          r>=u_sigma,
    Ctail_sigma=B_sigma^2+v*(2B_sigma-kappa).      (2.1)

For negative x, substituting t=-z in the integral gives
f(-r)=integral_0^r G_-(z)dz; hence the energy uses the ACTUAL
terminal-side cap, not the cap of an earlier source. Values and
derivatives agree at1 and at the ray's own u_sigma. In particular

    2F_(sigma_S)(r)=2K*r-[K^2+v*(2K-kappa)]
       for r>=v+K.                               (2.2)

The signed derivative g is continuous and nondecreasing, with
piecewise slopes kappa,1,0. Partitioning any real interval at
its finitely many knots proves that its derivative increment lies
between zero and the interval length. Thus g is globally
1-Lipschitz, f is finite, differentiable, convex, and 1-smooth.
Every G_sigma(r)>0 for r>0, so0 is its unique minimizer and f(0)=0.
For every ray and radius,

    0<=G_sigma(r)<=min(r,B_sigma).                 (2.3)

Every point used below is generated by the original GD recursion on
this one fixed f. In particular every query and the true terminal
have compatible function values and derivatives. For an explicit
interpolation check, if x<y and Delta=g(y)-g(x), then
0<=Delta<=y-x and

    g(t)-g(x)>=max(0,Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t)>=max(0,Delta-(t-x)),  x<=t<=y.

Integration bounds both corresponding increments below by
Delta^2/2. Equivalently, every ordered pair of actual points i,j
obeys

    f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2<=0.

This also includes the minimizer. Repeated points have identical
jets; scalar Gram matrices are positive semidefinite because
their quadratic forms are squares. No separate children or
unrealized terminal jets have been prescribed.

## 3. Same-m mass estimates and physical tree interfaces

The only numerical bounds needed in the two arguments are

    12/5<c<17/7<5/2,
    0<a<b<3/7<1/2,
    a<5/12,
    A_j>d_j/b for every 0<=j<=m.                  (3.1)

Here is a direct derivation. Since epsilon<1/1000,
sqrt(2)>7/5, sqrt(2)<17/12, and
(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7, the bounds on c follow.
The identity lambda*(lambda-2)=1 and c>lambda give
c*(c-2)>1>beta, hence a=beta/c<b. Since c>12/5
and beta<1, a<5/12. Also b=c-2<3/7<1/2.
Set eta=a/b. Then0<eta<1. The literal word masses satisfy

    A_0=1, A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j,
    A_j=(1-eta)*2^j+eta*c^j.                     (3.2)

The last expression has the correct initial value; substituting
it in the recurrence uses 2eta+a=c*eta. It is strictly larger
than eta*c^j=d_j/b. In particular at j=0 it gives a<b,
and for j>=1 one has A_j>=A_1=2+a>2.

Every physical level-k bridge in H_j is the middle entry of
an actual subword (H_k,1+d_k,H_k). Its full chronological
prefix and suffix within H_j therefore have masses at least
S_k. Immediately after the bridge lies its entire right H_k.
The rest of the suffix is exactly

    H_k, (1+d_(l_1)),H_(l_1), ...,
         (1+d_(l_t)),H_(l_t),
    k<l_1<...<l_t<=j-1.                          (3.3)

To see this, ascend the word tree from the bridge occurrence.
Each ancestor reached through a left child contributes its
bridge and right child; each reached through a right child
contributes no remaining entries. This includes an empty
ancestor list, skipped levels, and H_0 being empty.

If a level-k bridge lies within H_l, then k<l. Writing M,N
for its prefix and suffix masses WITHIN that H_l gives

    M,N>=S_k,
    S_l=M+(1+d_k)+N.                             (3.4)

These are identities for physical entries, not recurrences
evaluated by a program or factors appended after the terminal.

## 4. Exact signed endpoint map and elementary trajectory payments

For completeness, retain both caps in the exact one-step interval
map. At a source on ray sigma, with h=1+d, d>=0, the signed
output relative to that source sign is

    T_sigma(r)=r-hG_sigma(r)
              =(1-h*kappa)r,          0<=r<=1,
                h*v-d*r,              1<=r<=u_sigma,
                r-h*B_sigma,          r>=u_sigma.

Writing [z]_+=max(z,0), its same-ray and opposite-ray maxima
over the actual source interval0<=r<=R are

    Stay_sigma(R)=max([1-h*kappa]_+*min(R,1),
                      [R-h*B_sigma]_+),
    Cross_sigma(R)=max([h*kappa-1]_+*min(R,1),
                       [d*min(R,u_sigma)-h*v]_+).  (4.1)

Each affine piece has extrema at its endpoints. The middle
piece decreases, so its positive maximum is already supplied
at the core endpoint, while its most negative value is at
min(R,u_sigma). The tail increases, so it supplies the final
same-side endpoint and the cap-knot crossing endpoint.
If R<1, the formal middle crossing value is dominated by the
core crossing value because their difference is h*v*(R-1)<=0.
If R<u_sigma, the formal tail stay is no larger than the
actual endpoint T_sigma(R), and if positive that actual stay
is already covered by the core endpoint. Thus absent pieces
do not introduce unattained maxima.

For a signed interval [-P,Q], P,Q>=0, the exact image is
[-Pnext,Qnext], where

    Qnext=max(Stay_+(Q),Cross_-(P)),
    Pnext=max(Stay_-(P),Cross_+(Q)).               (4.2)

The map is continuous and fixes0, so the attained extrema
give its entire interval image. This is a two-endpoint map,
with the two independently fixed caps. No odd radius-map
substitution is made. The proofs below need only its actual
trajectory consequences; they do not execute this map.

If a strict crossing at a level-k bridge has source radius y,
source gradient magnitude p=G_sigma(y), and landing radius z,
then exactly

    z=(1+d_k)p-y<=d_k*p.                         (4.3)

This uses p<=y from (2.3) and keeps the source and destination
rays separate. In particular a small-to-large crossing has
z<=d_k*K, while a large-to-small crossing need not have that
bound with K in place of p.

On a segment with no strict crossing and a nonzero endpoint,
there is no zero hit. All points stay on one ray, all radii
decrease, and all gradient magnitudes decrease. If it starts
at radius Z, ends at radius r with gradient magnitude p,
and its full mass is U, then

    Z>=r+U*p>=(1+U)*p.                           (4.4)

The second inequality uses p<=r. If r>=v+B_sigma, every point
of this segment is on that same tail, so it has the stronger
identity Z=r+U*B_sigma.

For later noncrossing full histories, integrate
G_sigma(t)<=min(t,p) for0<=t<=r to obtain

    2F_sigma(r)<=2r*p-p^2.

If the whole H_m is noncrossing, R>=r+S_m*p, so

    2f(x_n)<=2R*p-D*p^2<=R^2/D.                 (4.5)

The last step follows from D*(p-R/D)^2>=0. This applies on
either ray and at either cap knot.

## 5. Mechanism 1: larger-ray capture from the last crossing

Take any complete H_j satisfying the premise of conclusion1.
Since the terminal is nonzero, no earlier point is zero. Choose
the LAST strict crossing, of physical level k. As its suffix
has no further crossing and ends on the larger ray, this crossing
is small-to-large. Its source gradient is at mostK, and by
(4.3) its landing z satisfies z<=d_k*K.

Let T be the mass of its ENTIRE remaining physical suffix.
Every point after the crossing stays on the larger ray; (4.4)
applied to that suffix and its terminal gradient p_n yields

    (1+T)*p_n<=z<=d_k*K.

The suffix contains its literal right H_k, so T>=S_k. Therefore

    p_n<=d_k*K/(1+T)<=d_k*K/A_k<b*K.

For k=0 the right child is empty, A_0=1, d_0=a<b; the same
formula holds without supplying a nonexistent contraction.
All ancestors in (3.3) are included in T. Earlier crossings,
of any signs and levels, are irrelevant to this last-crossing
payment; they have not been assumed absent.

Because b<1 and K<B, (1.1) also implies p_n<B, so this
larger-ray terminal is strictly before ITS OWN cap. This
does not imply any comparable radius statement on the small
ray. In particular |g|<=K there allows an arbitrarily long
linear tail without a separate history payment.

This direct sign-sensitive physical-suffix proof establishes
the frozen state without an auxiliary amplitude induction.
There is no q=-1 issue, changed grid cap, replaced curvature,
or use of c*(c-2)=1. In fact c*(c-2)>1 was retained in (3.1).
No unverified prior-child capture is an induction premise.

## 6. Mechanism 2, first interface: a prefix-suffix mass product

For any physical level-k bridge within H_l, k<l, let M,N
be its prefix and suffix masses within that literal child.
We claim the strict inequality

    (1+M)*(1+N)>d_l*d_k.                          (6.1)

This is a new intermediate derivation in the SAME joint
unverified mechanism, not a separately accepted lemma.

Put X=1+M, Y=1+N, and A=A_k. Equations(3.4) give

    X,Y>=A, X+Y=A_l-d_k.

Thus (X-A)(Y-A)>=0 gives

    X*Y>=A*(A_l-d_k-A).                          (6.2)

The sum condition also gives A<=(A_l-d_k)/2, hence

    A_l-d_k-A >= (A_l-d_k)/2.

Since l>=k+1, d_k<=d_l/c, and (3.1) gives A_l>d_l/b.
It follows that

    A_l-d_k-A > d_l*(1/b-1/c)/2
                > (3/4)*d_l > b*d_l.            (6.3)

For the middle strict inequality use b<1/2 and c>2, so
1/b>2 and 1/c<1/2. For the final one use b<1/2<3/4.
Finally A=A_k>d_k/b. Combining (6.2)-(6.3) proves
X*Y>d_k*d_l. It includes k=0 and both extremal positions
of the bridge within its containing word. No symmetry of
the actual iterates or equal prefix/suffix masses is needed.

## 7. Every smaller-tail terminal with crossings has only one

Suppose the actual H_m terminal lies on the smaller tail:
x_n=sigma_S*r_n with r_n>=v+K. Suppose first that there
is at least one crossing. Let its LAST crossing have level k,
source radius y on the larger ray, gradient magnitude p,
and landing z on the smaller ray. Let T be its complete
remaining suffix mass. There are no further crossings,
so all subsequent radii decrease to r_n and all are at
least v+K. Therefore every subsequent queried gradient has
magnitudeK, and

    z=r_n+T*K >= v+(1+T)*K >= A_k*K.

Together with (4.3), this gives the necessary inequality

    p> K/b.                                      (7.1)

Indeed d_k*p>=z>=A_k*K and A_k/d_k>1/b.

Assume for contradiction that there is an earlier crossing.
Choose the PENULTIMATE crossing. It is small-to-large;
write l for its physical level and L for its landing radius.
Its source cap is K, so (4.3) gives

    L<=d_l*K.                                    (7.2)

Between these two consecutive crossings every iterate stays
on the larger ray. There are exactly two chronological cases.

**Case A: the last crossing is after the entire immediate
right H_l of the penultimate bridge has finished.**
This H_l has no crossing. Its endpoint gradient q satisfies
(4.4), including l=0 where the child is empty:

    q<=L/A_l<=d_l*K/A_l<b*K.

All later larger-ray points before the last crossing have
nonincreasing radius and gradient. Thus its source p<=q<bK.
This contradicts (7.1), since b<1. This argument pays the
entire child from the actual penultimate landing; it does
not reset an original denominator.

**Case B: the last crossing lies inside that immediate
right H_l.**
Its level k is then smaller than l. Let M,N be the literal
prefix and suffix masses of this bridge INSIDE this H_l.
Before the last crossing there is no intervening crossing,
so (4.4) applied from L to its source gives

    L>=y+M*p>=(1+M)*p.                           (7.3)

After the last crossing every remaining original iterate,
in particular the endpoint of this H_l, lies on the smaller
tail. Denote this child's endpoint radius by r_child.
It satisfies r_child>=r_n>=v+K>=K, and the N intervening
mass has constant gradient magnitudeK. Consequently

    z=r_child+N*K >=(1+N)*K.

Combine this with z<=d_k*p, (7.3), and (7.2). All factors
are positive, so

    (1+M)*(1+N)*K <=d_k*(1+M)*p
                   <=d_k*L<=d_k*d_l*K.

Division byK>0 contradicts (6.1).

Both cases are impossible. Thus there cannot be a
penultimate crossing. The last crossing is the FIRST and
ONLY strict crossing of the original trajectory, and its
initial ray must be the larger ray. All original earlier
queries stay on that ray with decreasing radii. This is a
derived property of every smaller-tail terminal history,
not an itinerary assumption imported from the prior root
example. In particular a start on the small ray cannot
leave and return to a smaller-tail terminal.

## 8. Original-radius payment at every possible crossing level

Continue with a smaller-tail terminal history that has
crossed. Section7 permits use of a FIRST-crossing price,
now with its full charge explicitly from the original R.
Let P,T be the total physical masses before and after its
unique level-k crossing. Let y,p,z be its actual source
radius, source gradient, and landing as in Section7. Then

    P,T>=S_k,
    S_m=P+(1+d_k)+T,
    R>=y+P*p,
    z=(1+d_k)*p-y,
    r_n=z-T*K.                                   (8.1)

The prefix inequality follows by summing EVERY original
earlier step: its larger-ray gradient is at least p. No
all-tail assumption about the larger-ray prefix is used.

By (7.1), p>K/b>K>=kappa. A core source would have
p<=kappa, so y>1. On the larger ray's exterior middle
y=v+p, and on its tail y>=v+p. Thus

    y>=v+p,
    R>=v+(P+1)*p,
    r_n<=d_k*p-v-T*K.                            (8.2)

At the actual smaller-tail terminal use the exact energy
(2.2), retaining its intercept before any bound:

    2f(x_n)=2K*((1+d_k)*p-y-T*K)
                -[K^2+v*(2K-kappa)].             (8.3)

Replacing y by its lower bound v+p gives

    2f(x_n)<=N0,
    N0=2d_k*p*K-(2T+1)*K^2-4v*K+v*kappa.         (8.4)

The left side is strictly positive because r_n>=v+K>0;
therefore N0>0 as well. As K>=kappa and v>=0,
-4vK+v*kappa<=0. Using the positive denominator lower
bounds in (8.2), then discarding this nonpositive numerator
term, yields

    2f(x_n)/R^2
      <= N0/[v+(P+1)*p]^2
      <= [2d_k*p*K-(2T+1)*K^2]/[(P+1)^2*p^2].    (8.5)

All denominator reductions apply to positive numerators.
With t=K/p>0, completion of the square

    2d_k*t-(2T+1)*t^2
       =d_k^2/(2T+1)
         -(2T+1)*(t-d_k/(2T+1))^2

gives the paid position-sensitive bound

    2f(x_n)/R^2 <= d_k^2/[(P+1)^2*(2T+1)].       (8.6)

The actual capB and source branch constrain feasible p,
but enlarging that feasible range in this square bound
only increases the upper bound. The source function,
start, and queries have not been changed.

## 9. Uniform same-m bound including the leaf case

Set X=P+1, Y=T+1, A=A_k, d=d_k. They satisfy

    X,Y>=A,
    D=2X+2Y+2d-1.

Multiplying (8.6) by D gives at most

    d^2*(2X+2Y+2d-1)/[X^2*(2Y-1)].              (9.1)

For fixed X this decreases as Y increases, since its
Y-dependent factor is 1+(2X+2d)/(2Y-1), with positive
denominator. Thus replace Y by A. For fixed A the
remaining X-dependent factor is

    [2X+(2A+2d-1)]/X^2,

which decreases on X>0 because it is2/X plus a positive
constant divided by X^2. Replacing X by A therefore gives

    D*2f(x_n)/R^2
      <= d^2*(4A+2d-1)/[A^2*(2A-1)].             (9.2)

This is a bound using the complete original D, not a
conversion from a child denominator after discarding
ancestors. It allows every bridge position simultaneously.

If k>=1, then A>2 and q=d/A<b<3/7. Hence

    d^2*(4A+2d-1)/[A^2*(2A-1)]
       =q^2*(4+2q-1/A)/(2-1/A)
       < (9/49)*(4+6/7)/(3/2)
       =204/343<3/5.                             (9.3)

If k=0, then A=1 and d=a<5/12, so instead

    d^2*(4A+2d-1)/[A^2*(2A-1)]
       =a^2*(3+2a)
       <(25/144)*(23/6)=575/864<2/3.             (9.4)

This treats the empty H_0 and its possibly first or last
physical leaf explicitly. Together (9.3)-(9.4) prove

    2f(x_n)/R^2<2/(3D)

for every crossing smaller-tail terminal history. Adding
the noncrossing estimate (4.5) proves conclusion2 for the
ENTIRE specified terminal class, with C0=0 and m>=1536.

Neither a first exterior crossing estimate nor an interior
child-input bound was charged directly to R before Section7
proved that all earlier crossings are absent. This resolves
the original-denominator issue rather than assuming it.

## 10. Boundary checks and scope of the new interfaces

The preceding arguments include kappa=1, where v=0; there
is no division by v. Either cap may equal kappa where
allowed (in particular K=kappa), and the middle piece on
that ray may be empty. A smaller terminal at exactly
r_n=v+K belongs to the tail class and has both matching
energy expressions. Every subsequent radius after the last
crossing is at least this threshold, so the constant-K
suffix identities still hold at equality. A radius1 is in
the closed core, but (7.1) strictly excludes it as the
source of a crossing with a smaller-tail terminal.

A zero hit is incompatible with a nonzero larger-ray
terminal or a smaller-tail terminal, so omitting zero in
the trajectory-payment arguments loses no relevant case.
The allowed initial ray is arbitrary in conclusion1 and
in conclusion2. The unique-crossing argument proves the
initial larger ray when crossings occur in conclusion2.

The selected bridges and the child containing the final
two crossings are actual physical occurrences in H_m.
All suffix mass statements include every remaining query
and the true terminal; none adds a terminal-only factor
or pretends that a skipped ancestor is present. Strict
A_k>d_k/b depends on the same enclosing m, never on a
replacement child value of epsilon or beta.

The inequality K/B<b is part of the frozen assigned
family, but these two particular arguments do not need
its full strength once K is designated the smaller cap.
This observation is only a scope check; no third family
or additional mechanism is developed.

The first mechanism now provides the desired two-ray
gradient state after crossings: p<bK on the larger ray,
p<=K on the smaller. The second pays all smaller-tail
terminals with their ACTUAL energy, without the invalid
replacement 2F_small<=p_terminal^2/kappa on that tail.

The following interfaces are still absent for a theorem
covering ALL starts in the separated-cap family:

1. A gradient or objective estimate charged to original R
   for crossed histories ending on the larger ray. Capture
   gives p<bK and ensures a pre-cap endpoint there, but
   K/R is not bounded by this statement at the required
   full-word scale. A uniform original-radius bound such
   as the balanced proof's global-gradient estimate has
   not been established here.
2. A corresponding estimate for crossed histories ending
   strictly before the smaller cap knot (in its core or
   middle). Their tail payment does not apply. The prior
   balanced grid and its cap-ratio-paid saturated steps
   cannot be imported at a separated cap ratio.
3. Even after those scalar interfaces, the full
   finite-dimensional W_n(H_m) upper bound required by
   accepted931b9653ce0f7201 would remain a separate
   theorem-strength requirement.

No failure or counterexample to either frozen candidate
has been found in this derivation. It proves neither
that all separated caps are harmless nor that the
original minimax conjecture is false. The conclusion
is precisely the two unverified scoped results above.

## 11. Provenance, retrieval, and bounded handoff

The worker TASK, latest applicable guidance44905a26e2e8dbff,
project PROBLEM, own continuation memory, relevant shared
findings/dead ends, and the complete prior report were read.
The accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 was read in full to retain
its CONDITIONAL full-W scope. It is not a premise for an
unproved step in the two arguments above. Project glossary
was checked; local symbols are explicitly scoped here.

Skills used: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving.
A focused Matlas search returned extragradient, momentum,
and stochastic-ADMM statements with incompatible dynamics.
Web fallback returned silver-schedule background rather
than the altered same-m separated-cap law. No external
theorem or paper is used as a premise. These arguments
remain in the previously examined piecewise-affine family
and introduce no new external technical machinery. No
additional paper premise is needed to reject a branch;
neither frozen candidate was rejected.

An early combined recall was truncated. Operative inputs
and all sections of the prior report were subsequently
read in bounded chunks. This is an administrative read
caveat, not mathematical evidence.

Resource use: zero mathematical programs, numerical or
arithmetic replay, executed trajectory/recurrence runs,
solvers, optimizers, benchmark DP, formal calls,
fact_submit, installations, consultations, or subagents.
Only permitted text retrieval, reasoning, local/shared
text persistence, and administrative integrity checks
were used. No live TASK edit, lifecycle action, outward

credit reuse, or Goal completion occurs.

The two authorized mechanisms are complete at JOINT
UNVERIFIED level. The report and HANDOFF are the return
at cap1, not authorization for another round. No new
