# Outside-base character initialization: three full-word analytical screens

Status: **COMPLETE JOINT CANDIDATE, UNVERIFIED**. Worker max2, allocated
invocation66 after ended baseline65; TASK.md and master_guidance
bd2b8e8fc46ee8bb. Exactly three static laws are studied. All new arguments
are supplied here, including the reused cap-return prefix. No claim below is
a fact, and no formal call is allocated. The original exact-profile target
remains OPEN.

The first law is the previously displayed two-character cap-return
initialization, now paid through the entire original hierarchy: it reaches a
minimum at query6 and its terminal value is zero. The second law adds a truly
independent coordinate with a different clipping response; it also reaches a
minimum at query6. The third is an arbitrary-rank acute full-sign pyramid
with outside-base initialization and a nonzero polar normal. Its old terminal
has an exact scalar Huber formula. Its maximal full-prefix completion has an
explicit terminal gradient/value, an exact complete minimum set and closest
distance. EVERY other legal full-prefix completion is separately paid and
has original-benchmark ratio below19/20 on the same full H_m.

Accepted inputs are fact_id1252b8823bb5a290 for ordered interpolation
necessity and the full finite-prefix conjugate construction, and
fact_id5fd396eba75651e7 ONLY for its scalar SAME-H_m Huber/original-B
comparison, proof Section10 equation(77). Its spiral-family optimum is not
an input for this geometry. No assertion from3a0ebc8f9657d654,
dd196fada42b1983 or ed67c28d3bc2fa01 is used as a premise.

## 1. Common complete real character body and actual function

For a positive integer q and positive widths b_1,...,b_q, take the full
group G=(Z/2Z)^q, coordinate characters chi_j(s)=(-1)^(s_j), and orbit
weights2^(-q). The weights specify the full orbit; no expected Gram or
averaged gradient is used. The metric on R^(q+1) is the identity. Every
real support slope and every offset is

    v_s=(1,b_1 chi_1(s),...,b_q chi_q(s)), offset_s=0,
    D=conv({0} union {v_s:s in G}),
    J(y)=max(0,y_0+sum_j b_j|y_j|),
    F(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

Thus the finite support law and the one global smooth function are completely
specified before any GD run. The hull and its generated cone are

    D={(t,w):0<=t<=1, |w_j|<=b_j t},
    C={(t,w):t>=0, |w_j|<=b_j t},
    C^polar={(u,z):u+sum_j b_j|z_j|<=0}.

For t>0 the coefficients
2^(-q) product_j[1+chi_j(s)w_j/(b_j t)] express w/t as a convex
combination of every base vertex: they are nonnegative, sum to1 and give
each required coordinate by summing the signs separately. The origin has
coefficient1-t for D. The inequalities for C and its polar then follow by
positive scaling and maximizing each transverse sign. This proves the
body's real realization and its exact rank q+1.

At an arbitrary query x=(u,z), its projection is completely determined by

    Psi_x(t)=t-u-sum_j b_j(|z_j|-b_j t)_+,
    g=P_D(x)=(t,clip(z_j,[-b_j t,b_j t])_(j=1,...,q)).

Use t=0 when Psi_x(0)>=0, t=1 when Psi_x(1)<=0, and otherwise the unique
root in(0,1). Psi is continuous and strictly increasing. In an interior
cell whose saturated coordinates are I, the root is the explicit number

    t=(u+sum_(j in I)b_j|z_j|)/(1+sum_(j in I)b_j^2),

with |z_j|/b_j>=t on I and <=t off I. This finite cell description includes
every sign and tie; ties give the same root by strict monotonicity. It is
an exact mathematical rule, not an executed trajectory or root oracle.
The origin, cap and each signed saturated lateral equality specify the
actual active face. Every original physical update is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i.

Here is a direct proof of the global function and every directed row. The
strictly concave maximization has unique maximizer g=P_D(x). Its full normal
condition is

    <x-g,w-g><=0 for every w in D.

Adding the two conditions at x,y gives
<x-y,P_D(x)-P_D(y)> >= ||P_D(x)-P_D(y)||^2. Consequently projection is
1-Lipschitz. Evaluating the maximum at the two maximizers bounds
F(y)-F(x)-<P_D(x),y-x> between0 and
<P_D(y)-P_D(x),y-x>. This proves differentiability with gradient P_D,
convexity, finiteness and global1-smoothness. D has nonempty interior, on
which projection is the identity, so the least smoothness is1.

For every actual sample, including the terminal and ANY minimum, put

    r_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=<r_i,g_i>,
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2.

The normal comparison with zero gives delta_i>=0. Direct expansion gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                            (1)

This proves BOTH orientations of every remote, adjacent, terminal and
minimum row. The whole Gram is a Gram of displayed real Euclidean vectors
and is PSD. No original query or gradient coordinate is omitted.

The complete minimum and value are

    min F=0, argmin F=C^polar.                        (2)

Every point of the polar makes all objectives nonpositive, and zero attains
zero. Outside the polar, a sufficiently small positive multiple of a vertex
with positive inner product gives a strictly positive objective. This proves
necessity as well as sufficiency.

## 2. One fixed full-prefix completion, all its anchors and all minima

For each law below retain EVERY old jet (x_i,g_i,f_i),0<=i<n, and the old
optimum jet(0,0,0). Define BEFORE running this completed function

    H_pre(u)=||u||^2/2+max(0,max_(0<=i<n){<r_i,u>-delta_i}),
    F_pre(x)=sup_u{<x,u>-H_pre(u)}.                   (3)

The old coefficients will be specified by closed formulas, including every
repeated minimum query. Formula(3) includes them all with weight1 in the
maximum. It is not a suffix reset or a switch of function during GD.

For these old projection jets the conjugate jet value is ||g_i||^2/2, and
the lower jet quadratic of accepted1252 reduces to
||u||^2/2+<r_i,u>-delta_i. Equation(1) checks every hypothesis of its finite
prefix construction. Fact_id1252b8823bb5a290 therefore gives one convex
differentiable1-smooth F_pre interpolating all the original prefix jets and
the optimum0. Induction over ALL physical indices shows its actual GD
reaches the old terminal position x_n. Its actual terminal gradient is the
unique maximizer in(3); it is never assigned the old projection gradient.
The accepted interpolation necessity proves every row of its actual
terminal and every one of its own minima in both orientations.

Its exact complete minimum is

    min F_pre=0,
    argmin F_pre=conv({0} union {r_i:0<=i<n,delta_i=0}). (4)

Indeed H_pre(0)=0, and F_pre(x)=0 iff <x,u><=H_pre(u) for all u, that is,
x is a subgradient of H_pre at zero. Every convex combination of the active
zero-offset slopes is such a subgradient. Conversely, a point outside their
convex hull can be strictly separated from it. In the separating direction,
at a sufficiently small positive scale the positive-offset terms remain
inactive and the quadratic is of smaller order, violating the subgradient
inequality. This proves(4), including all zero-height old normals.

Let Kfun be ANY other static convex differentiable1-smooth function that
interpolates the entire same old prefix and the optimum0. It has minimum0
and the same actual physical prefix and terminal POSITION. Accepted1252
gives Kfun<=F_pre pointwise. Its closest initial distance must nevertheless
be paid separately. In particular, the outgoing row of the LAST ORIGINAL
query, with its actual terminal gradient unrestricted, gives

    Kfun(x_n)<=delta_(n-1)+||x_n-r_(n-1)||^2/2.        (5)

This follows by completing the terminal-gradient square in Q_(n,n-1).
No incoming row is silently discarded in the actual realizing function.
Equations(3)--(5) also apply to F_pre itself. The exact distances below do
not transfer a radius between different functions.

## 3. The unchanged full hierarchy and the original benchmark

Use the SAME enclosing parameters at every depth:

    m>=1536, lambda=1+sqrt(2), e=1/(4m), beta=1-e,
    c=lambda(1+e), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i.

All physical steps are >1. The full word begins

    l,k,l,H,l,k,l,
    l=1+a, k=1+beta, H=1+beta*c.                     (6)

This is the actual first H_3 child of the original H_m, without resetting m
or suppressing any remaining step. Its last physical step is l. Since
every step exceeds1, S>n>600 for the declared m. Define A_i=sum_(j<i)h_j;
these are original physical partial masses, not an executed recurrence.

Some transparent exact bounds used below are

    241/100<lambda<121/50, 0<e<1/1000,
    999/1000<beta<1,
    41/100<a<5/12,
    141/100<l<17/12,
    17/5<H<171/50.                                  (7)

The lambda enclosure follows by squaring141/100 and142/100 around sqrt2.
For the lower a bound, c<(121/50)(1001/1000)=121121/50000, so
a>49950/121121>41/100; the last comparison is
4995000>4965961. For the upper bound use a<1/lambda<100/241<5/12.
Finally beta*c=lambda(1-e^2); the lower lambda and e bounds put H above
17/5, and H<1+lambda<171/50. Thus the bounds are uniform over all m here.

The original benchmark retains ALL integer splits:

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    Gbench(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)Gbench(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1, U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.               (8)

These are definitions, not a dynamic program run in this round. The only
benchmark inequality imported from fact_id5fd396eba75651e7, equation(77), is

    B_(2^m-1)/(1+2S)<237/250, m>=1536, tau=1/4.      (9)

It is used ONLY as this scalar same-word comparison. Its original all-split
denominator and its direction are retained.

## 4. Laws A and B: real slopes, initializations and all actual queries

LAW A has q=1,b_1=3, the two slopes(1,3),(1,-3), offsets0, weights1/2,
and the body/function from Section1 on R^2. Set T=2l+k and

    x_0=(T,3(1+T)).

Its initial transverse coordinate exceeds its base width3.

LAW B has q=2,(b_1,b_2)=(3,1), all FOUR real slopes
(1,3sigma_1,sigma_2),sigma_j in{-1,1}, offsets0, weights1/4,
and the body/function from Section1 on R^3. Fix ANY |theta|<=1/2 and

    x_0=(T,3(1+T),theta).

The first coordinate is outside its base. The second has an independent
initial ratio, and its gradient follows its own signed physical factors;
for theta!=0 these gradients are not on one ray. This is not the equal-ratio
lift of the two-character run. The metric is the identity in both laws.
Every parameter is chosen before the one full run on H_m.

We give the complete textual derivation for B; deleting the last coordinate
gives every statement for A. Only the actual first six original steps are
used to prove absorption; ALL remaining steps are then accounted for by a
zero gradient on the same fixed function.

Define these six literal signed factors, with no executed product:

    pi_0=1, pi_1=-a, pi_2=a beta, pi_3=-a^2 beta,
    pi_4=beta^4/c, pi_5=-beta^5/c^2, pi_6=beta^6/c^2.

For i=0,1,2 set C_i=T-A_i. Directly C_0=T,C_1=l+k,C_2=l, all >1. The
actual query, projection and value are

    x_i=(C_i,3(1+C_i),pi_i theta),
    g_i=(1,3,pi_i theta),
    f_i=10C_i+4+pi_i^2 theta^2/2,
    r_i=(C_i-1,3C_i,0), delta_i=10C_i-1>0.           (10)

The second transverse coordinate is inside its cap interval, since its
absolute value is at most1/2 at these queries. The first transverse
coordinate is above3, and Psi_x(1)=1-10C_i<0. These are actual cap
projections with their signs. Subtracting the original l,k,l gives

    x_3=(0,3,pi_3 theta),
    g_3=(9/10,27/10,pi_3 theta),
    r_3=(-9/10,3/10,0), delta_3=0,
    f_3=81/20+pi_3^2 theta^2/2.                     (11)

Here |pi_3 theta|<25/288<9/10. The projected carrier9/10 is below the cap,
the first positive lateral side is active, and the second coordinate is
unsaturated. The displayed r_3 belongs to the full polar and is orthogonal
to g_3, proving its full projection normal condition, including all FOUR
sign planes. The cap is strictly inactive at this actual query.

The NEXT ORIGINAL step is H, giving

    x_4=(-9H/10,3-27H/10,pi_4 theta),
    g_4=(1,-3,pi_4 theta),
    r_4=(-9H/10-1,6-27H/10,0),
    delta_4=(36/5)H-19>0,
    f_4=(36/5)H-14+pi_4^2 theta^2/2.                (12)

Indeed |pi_4 theta|<1/(2lambda)<1, r_(4,1)<0, and
r_(4,0)-3r_(4,1)=(36/5)H-19>0. The maximum of <r_4,g> over the FULL D
is exactly this last positive number, attained at carrier1, first transverse
coordinate-3 and any allowed second coordinate. Thus the displayed g_4 is
the actual projection. The cap has reactivated, with the opposite lateral
side, rather than through a prescribed gradient switch.

After the NEXT ORIGINAL leaf l put

    d=(18/25)H-9/10-l.

Bounds(7) give

    13/100<d<4/25.                                  (13)

For the lower bound,
d>306/125-9/10-17/12=197/1500>13/100.
For the upper bound,
d<3078/1250-231/100=381/2500<4/25.
The exact query and gradient now are

    x_5=(-9H/10-l,3-27H/10+3l,pi_5 theta),
    g_5=(d,-3d,pi_5 theta),
    r_5=(9/10-81H/50,3/10-27H/50,0), delta_5=0,
    f_5=5d^2+pi_5^2 theta^2/2.                      (14)

Both the first carrier and first transverse query coordinates are negative:
the latter is less than3-459/50+17/4<0. Also
|pi_5 theta|<1/(2lambda^2)<9/100<d. Consequently the second coordinate
remains unsaturated on this cone face. The r_5 first transverse coordinate
is negative and r_(5,0)-3r_(5,1)=0. Hence r_5 lies in the full polar and
<r_5,g_5>=0. Together with0<d<1 this proves the entire actual projection
on the negative lateral side with strictly inactive cap.

After the NEXT ORIGINAL level1 bridge k=1+beta,

    x_6=(9/10-81H/50-beta*d,
         3/10-27H/50+3beta*d,
         pi_6 theta).                              (15)

Its first transverse coordinate is negative: by(7),(13) it is less than
3/10-459/250+12/25=-264/250<0. Therefore its COMPLETE polar inequality is

    (x_6)_0+3|(x_6)_1|+|(x_6)_2|
       =-10beta*d+|pi_6 theta|<0,                   (16)

because beta>999/1000,d>13/100, whereas
|pi_6 theta|<1/(2lambda^2)<9/100. This pays the second-coordinate sign
change as well. For A delete the last term in(16).

Thus g_6=0 and f_6=0. EVERY remaining ORIGINAL step, including the next
leaf and all later bridges at all depths, leaves this same x_6 unchanged.
The ACTUAL full terminal is

    x_n=x_6, g_n=0, f_n=0.                          (17)

This proves the full unbounded-depth result with every repeated cap visit
and every late query included. It is not extrapolation from a finite sample:
the actual queried minimum absorbs all remaining updates on the fixed F.

## 5. Laws A/B: complete old and new minima, exact true radii, all completions

Let t_A=T+9/10. For B, the actual closest old minimum is r_3 and

    x_0-r_3=(t_A,3t_A,theta),
    rho_old^2=10t_A^2+theta^2.                      (18)

The difference belongs to C because |theta|<=1/2<t_A. The r_3 is in
C^polar and is orthogonal to that difference. For any other polar point y,
<x_0-r_3,y-r_3><=0; expansion of ||x_0-y||^2 proves that r_3 is the unique
closest point. For A this gives the moved closest minimum(-9/10,3/10)
and rho_old^2=10t_A^2. Origin is not used as a replacement radius.

All zero-offset full-prefix normals are exactly r_3,r_5 and x_6, with
x_6 repeated at EVERY original query i>=6. Equations(10),(12) show every
other retained normal has strictly positive offset. Thus the exact completed
minimum in(4) is

    argmin F_pre=conv{0,r_3,r_5,x_6}, min F_pre=0.     (19)

Every displayed point is in C^polar and r_3 is included, so the same
nearest-point argument gives rho_pre^2=10t_A^2+theta^2, or10t_A^2 for A.
Its actual GD has the queried zero gradient at index6, so its terminal
gradient/value are exactly zero as well. The complete maximum in(3) retains
ALL positive-height cap normals and ALL zero-height repeated indices;
equation(19) identifies active terms at zero, not a deletion from the function.

For ANY other legal full-prefix completion Kfun, r_3 is a minimum:
its old query3 satisfies f_3=||g_3||^2/2 and x_3-g_3=r_3. Smoothness gives
Kfun(r_3)<=f_3-||g_3||^2/2=0; min Kfun=0 follows from its retained optimum.
For ANY minimum y the old initial and query1 optimum rows from(1) force

    <g_0,y><=0, <g_1,y><=0.

The reason is the exact old identity f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, which reduces
Q_(i,y) to <g_i,y>. Moreover

    x_0-r_3=alpha*g_0+gamma*g_1,
    alpha=(1+a*t_A)/(1+a)>0,
    gamma=(t_A-1)/(1+a)>0.                          (20)

The first two coordinates use alpha+gamma=t_A; the second independent
coordinate uses alpha-a*gamma=1. Also <g_0,r_3>=<g_1,r_3>=0. Hence
<x_0-r_3,y-r_3><=0 for every minimum y of Kfun. Its OWN exact closest
distance is therefore also(18). This is a proof for each completion, not
transfer of F or F_pre's radius.

The old query6 jet is (x_6,0,0); Kfun interpolates it and every remaining
repeated original jet. Its actual terminal is consequently also x_6 with
value0 and gradient0. Laws A/B and EVERY full-prefix completion have payoff
zero on the entire original H_m, and hence original-B ratio zero.

## 6. Law C: arbitrary-rank acute body with outside-base normal memory

Take the full character body in Section1 with ANY finite q>=1, ANY positive
widths satisfying

    K_width=sum_j b_j^2<=1, M=1+K_width.

Write v=v_(0,...,0)=(1,b_1,...,b_q). Choose t>1 and nonnegative
eta_1,...,eta_q, not all zero, satisfying

    sum_j b_j^2 eta_j<=t-1.

Define the nonzero polar normal and the initial point by

    r_p=(-sum_j b_j^2 eta_j,b_1 eta_1,...,b_q eta_q),
    x_0=r_p+t*v
       =(t-sum_j b_j^2 eta_j,b_j(t+eta_j)_(j=1,...,q)),
    N=||r_p||^2=(sum_j b_j^2 eta_j)^2+sum_j b_j^2 eta_j^2>0.

The initial carrier is at least1, and EVERY transverse coordinate exceeds
its corresponding base width. The rank, widths, all unequal eta_j, and t
may depend on the full known word. They are fixed before the function/GD.
There is no within-base retuning. For example q=2,b_1=1/2,b_2=sqrt(3)/2,
eta_1=t-1,eta_2=(t-1)/2 is a genuinely unequal-normal initialization within
this single parameterized law.

Directly r_p belongs to C^polar and <r_p,v>=0. For every sign vertex w,

    <r_p,w><=0,
    <v,w>=1+sum_j b_j^2 chi_j(w)>=1-K_width>=0.      (21)

For EVERY real scalar s the exact old projection/function along the whole
affine line is

    P_D(r_p+s*v)=clip(s,[0,1])*v,
    F(r_p+s*v)=M*Hub_1(s),                           (22)

where Hub_1(s)=0 for s<=0, s^2/2 for0<=s<=1, and s-1/2 for s>=1.
For s<=0, (21) puts r_p+s*v in the full polar. For0<s<1 the feasible
projection s*v has residual r_p in the polar, orthogonal to s*v, hence its
normal condition holds. For s>=1 use g=v: for any sign vertex w,
<r_p+(s-1)v,w-v>=<r_p,w>+(s-1)(<v,w>-M)<=0, and the origin comparison is
-(s-1)M<=0. Convex combinations prove the normal condition for the full D.
Evaluation of F gives(22), including both equality endpoints.

At s=t the cone projection is t*v with residual r_p, so the actual closest
old minimum is r_p and

    rho_old^2=M*t^2.                                (23)

This follows directly from polar orthogonality: for any minimum y,
<t*v,y-r_p><=0. The squared-distance expansion gives uniqueness and(23).
The large normal component N is NOT charged as part of the old radius;
it will be charged correctly when the completed minimum changes.

## 7. Law C: full closed query map and exact old terminal

For all original physical steps, (22) gives x_i=r_p+s_i*v and
g_i=clip(s_i,[0,1])*v. These are derived gradients of the fixed F.
Their entire scalar history has a closed description. Until the first
original index k with t-A_k<1, s_i=t-A_i. At that index s_k=t-A_k.
If s_k<=0 it is frozen for all later original indices. If0<s_k<1 and k<n,
then s_(k+1)=(1-h_k)s_k<0 and it is frozen from k+1 onward. If k=n there
is no extra step. If no such index occurs through n, all s_i=t-A_i.
The equality s_i=1 remains the capped case and the next original step>1
puts it strictly below zero. This includes all late exits and every bridge.

Since EVERY original h_i>1, this description proves the exact terminal
value for the full H_m, and in fact for any nonempty word with all steps>1:

    F(x_n)=M*Hub_1(t-S).                             (24)

When t>S every old queried s_i is >1, because its remaining physical mass
includes the final step>1; hence s_n=t-S. When t<=S, either an earlier
queried minimum absorbs the rest, an interior queried ray state is killed
by its next original step, or the terminal first leaves the cap with
s_n=t-S<=0. All three possibilities have value zero.

The OLD actual closest-distance payoff, in the W=2R convention, is

    V_old=2Hub_1(t-S)/t^2,
    sup_(all Law C parameters)V_old=1/(1+2S).        (25)

For0<t-S<=1 the score is at most1/(S+1)^2<=1/(1+2S). For t-S>=1,

    t^2/(1+2S)-(2t-2S-1)=(t-(1+2S))^2/(1+2S)>=0.

Attainment uses t=1+2S and any allowed nonzero eta vector. It retains all
outside-base coordinates and its moved closest minimum. Thus by(9) the old
full family has original-B ratio strictly below237/250.

## 8. Law C completion: every coefficient, the whole minimum and true radius

Use the fixed complete maximum(3), retaining all original old indices and
the optimum0. In its coefficients put

    alpha_i=clip(s_i,[0,1]),
    r_i=r_p+(s_i-alpha_i)*v,
    delta_i=M*(s_i-alpha_i)*alpha_i.                 (26)

The entire closed s_i description in Section7 fixes all these coefficients
before the completed run. In particular every later absorbed jet is kept.
The complete minimum in(4) and its EXACT closest distance are as follows.

If any queried s_i belongs to[0,1], its zero-offset normal is r_p; all
other zero-offset normals are in the original polar. Therefore

    r_p in argmin F_pre, rho_pre^2=M*t^2.            (27)

The proof of nearestness is the same polar normal calculation as in(23),
now applied to the actual full convex hull in(4). Its complete minimum is
still that full hull, including any later absorbing point and zero.

If no queried s_i belongs to[0,1] but a minimum is queried, the first such
query has some s_abs<0. All later queries are the identical old minimum.
Thus the ENTIRE completed minimum is the segment

    [0,r_p+s_abs*v].

The exact nearest coefficient on that segment is

    zeta=max(0,(N+M*t*s_abs)/(N+M*s_abs^2))<1,
    rho_pre^2=M*t^2+N
           -(max(0,N+M*t*s_abs))^2/(N+M*s_abs^2).    (28)

The quotient is <1 because its numerator minus denominator is
M*s_abs*(t-s_abs)<0. This proves the full closest distance, rather than
selecting either segment endpoint as a convenient optimum.

If all queried s_i>1 there is NO old zero-offset normal, so

    argmin F_pre={0}, rho_pre^2=M*t^2+N.             (29)

These are exhaustive, including all equality cases through(27). In
particular all-cap completion generally has a DIFFERENT radius from the
old F: its entire normal component N is now part of the true distance.

For ANY OTHER legal full-prefix completion Kfun, the initial gradient is v
and its old value is M(t-1/2). For each of its OWN minima y, the initial
ordered optimum row gives <v,y><=0. Since <v,x_0>=M*t, projection on this
halfspace yields the separately proved lower bound

    dist(x_0,argmin Kfun)^2>=M*t^2.                  (30)

If a queried s_i belongs to(0,1], its old value is ||g_i||^2/2, so a unit
gradient step and smoothness force r_p to be a minimum of Kfun. In that
case(30) is equality. No equality is claimed without this argument. Only
the lower bound is needed for the uniform comparison below.

## 9. Law C: the ACTUAL completed terminal gradient and value

If any old queried gradient is zero, the actual GD of F_pre and EVERY
other complete-prefix completion is absorbed there and has terminal0.
There is no near-end exception to absorption at a queried minimum.

Otherwise the only possible interior-ray query is the LAST ORIGINAL one:
if 0<s_(n-1)<=1, put w=-a*s_(n-1). The exact completed terminal is

    x_n=r_p+w*v,
    grad F_pre(x_n)=w*v,
    F_pre(x_n)=M*w^2/2.                             (31)

For proof, the last zero-offset anchor is <r_p,u>, and all old cap anchors
have the form <r_p,u>+(s_i-1)(<v,u>-M) with s_i>=1. At u=w*v they are
nonpositive, the last one and optimum0 attain zero, and the objective is
M*w^2/2. Equation(5) gives that same upper value because delta_last=0 and
x_n-r_last=w*v. Strict concavity identifies the unique gradient. This
proves the actual completion formula even though the old F has terminal0.

Finally suppose the last old query is capped with s_(n-1)>=1. Then ALL
old prefix queries are capped. Put

    w=t-S, d=s_(n-1)-1=w+a>=0,
    R_last=r_p+d*v,
    p=max(0,1-M*d*l/(N+M*d^2)), 0<=p<=1,
    u_n=(w-p*d)*v+(1-p)*r_p.                        (32)

The denominator is positive since N>0. The actual completed terminal
gradient and value are the FULL formulas

    grad F_pre(x_n)=u_n,
    F_pre(x_n)=[M*(w-p*d)^2+N*(1-p)^2]/2+p*M*d.      (33)

In particular its gradient can have a component in the OLD polar-normal
direction. This freedom is included, not silently set to the old cap jet.

Here is the complete proof that no remote old anchor is lost. Write
D_first=t-1; every original cap coefficient s_i-1 lies in[d,D_first].
If a candidate u has <v,u><M, every such affine anchor is at most the last
one <R_last,u>-M*d. Thus the last one controls the entire retained cap
maximum in this region, without a step/anchor deletion.

Consider the single last-anchor function

    H_one(u)=||u||^2/2+max(0,<R_last,u>-M*d).

At X=x_n=r_p+w*v, completing its strictly convex maximization gives the
unique optimizer X-p*R_last, with

    p=clip((N+M*d*(w-1))/(N+M*d^2),[0,1]).

Because w=d-a, this is exactly(32). This statement can also be checked
without a minimax theorem: p minimizes the scalar quadratic
||X-p*R_last||^2/2+p*M*d on[0,1], and its endpoint/interior derivative
conditions are exactly the hinge subgradient conditions at u=X-p*R_last.

Its coefficient along v is strictly less than1. If p=0, the condition
N+M*d*(d-l)<=0 and N>0 imply d<l, so w=d-a<1. If p>0, direct algebra gives

    w-p*d=(-a*N+M*d^2)/(N+M*d^2)<1.                 (34)

The d=0 case has p=1 and coefficient-a, also covered by(34). Hence all
EARLIER cap anchors are indeed dominated by the last one AT THIS ACTUAL
unique optimizer. H_pre>=H_one everywhere and is equal there; their
conjugate values and optimizers at X coincide. Substitution proves(33).
This pays the whole original maximum and the terminal for all horizons,
including exact boundary queries and the unqueried terminal first exit.

Together(27)--(29),(31)--(33) give the full ACTUAL closest-distance payoff
of the fixed completed law: it is2F_pre(x_n)/rho_pre^2 with both quantities
given explicitly, without combining an old radius with a new terminal.

## 10. Law C: a full-family deficit for ALL legal completions

The exact completed formulas are stronger than needed for the following
uniform bound, which includes ALL other static full-prefix completions and
their OWN minima, in any finite ambient dimension containing the old jets.

If a queried minimum occurs the terminal is zero. If the last query has
0<s_(n-1)<=1, equation(5) and(30) give

    V(Kfun)<=a^2*s_(n-1)^2/t^2
            <=a^2/(S-a)^2.                          (35)

The second inequality uses t=S-l+s_(n-1) and S>l: the quotient
s/(S-l+s) increases for s in(0,1]. If the last query is capped, equation(5)
and(30), with the ACTUAL last leaf retained, give

    V(Kfun)<= (2t-2S+2a+a^2)/t^2
            <=1/(2S-2a-a^2).                       (36)

For the last inequality set D_pay=2S-2a-a^2>0 and expand
t^2/D_pay-(2t-D_pay)=(t-D_pay)^2/D_pay>=0.
This is an upper bound on the actual terminal value of each completion,
not a prescription for its gradient. It includes the fixed F_pre.

The bound in(35) is no larger than that in(36), since

    (S-a)^2-a^2*(2S-2a-a^2)=(S-a-a^2)^2>=0.

Therefore the COMPLETE stated acute outside-base family, including arbitrary
rank, unequal widths, all allowed normal offsets and initial t, the original
F, the fixed F_pre and EVERY legal complete-prefix completion, satisfies

    V<=1/(2S-2a-a^2).                               (37)

The old subfamily has the exact optimum in(25). For the union including all
completions its supremum lies between1/(1+2S) and the upper bound(37);
equality in(37) is NOT asserted.

Use a<1/2,S>600 to obtain

    2S-2a-a^2>2S-5/4>474*(9/4),
    (1+2S)/(2S-2a-a^2)
         =1+(1+a)^2/(2S-2a-a^2)<475/474.

Combining ONLY the accepted scalar comparison(9) with(37) proves the
strict original ALL-split benchmark deficit

    B_(2^m-1)*V<(237/250)*(475/474)=19/20,
    for EVERY m>=1536 and EVERY completion in the declared family. (38)

For the old actual F the stronger original ratio<237/250 follows from(25).
For laws A/B every completed/full-run ratio is zero. Hence the union of
these THREE stated laws and their arbitrary complete-prefix completions
has original-B ratio uniformly below19/20 on this same H_m diagnostic.

The normalization here is V=2(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 at advertised
smoothness1, so it is the project's W=2R convention. For arbitrary L>0,
use L*F, L*F_pre or L*Kfun and steps h_i/L. The points, gradients divided
by L and normalized V are unchanged. No rate symbol is used for a radius.

## 11. Exact boundary test and what remains open

The acute condition in Law C is essential to its scalar line rule. Law A
has K_width=9 and can be written in precisely the normal form

    v=(1,3), r_p=(-9/10,3/10), t=T+9/10,
    x_0=r_p+t*v.

After actual query3 with scalar coefficient9/10, the ORIGINAL H step gives
x_4=r_p+(9/10)(1-H)*v with negative scalar coefficient. Nonetheless its
ACTUAL projection is the opposite capped vertex(1,-3), as jointly proved
in(12). Thus removing K_width<=1 from(22) is concretely refuted by the SAME
static function, full original word and paid closest distance. A polar
normal alone does not imply absorption on the negative affine ray. The
subsequent genuine absorption at query6 does not undo this scope test.

The present full deficits exclude ONLY A, B and the acute normal/ray
family C, with their full-prefix completions. They do not exclude arbitrary
outside-base starts at K_width>1, multiple independent outside ratios in
obtuse/growing-rank bodies, dependent character dictionaries, changed
metrics/offsets or other functions. No unrestricted W upper bound, general
finite-field impossibility or arbitrary-word/horizon lower cover follows.

The remaining theorem-level interface is an actual outside-base law beyond
these scopes that survives the ENTIRE unchanged word with a useful
terminal/original-B payoff, followed by the separate original all-word
uniformity argument. Local cap returns, local value increases and the
projection threshold alone are insufficient. No fourth law or new round is
allocated here.

## 12. Literature, recall, glossary and resource record

Query-memory recalled the full mandatory3a/dd/ed findings and exact old
private handoff. The older acute-transverse candidate was read as awareness;
it supplies no premise here. Fact search found no accepted outside-base
character theorem. Accepted1252 was read in full and accepted5fd at its

# Two fixed curvatures: exact original-word dynamics and a uniform family deficit

Status: ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED draft, max3 invocation73, 2026-10-02.
No statement below is a new accepted fact. No fact_submit or other formal call
was authorized or made. Original exact-profile problem: OPEN.

TASK.md and master_guidance efacaf1e71778e76 authorize one analytical
invocation, baseline72, ended ceiling73/attempted74, cap1, at most two laws.
This report examines ONE law only: the requested static two-sided quadratic.
It permits every actual overlapping return and every within-block crossing.
There is no reset, changing derivative, disjointness hypothesis, or omitted
physical step. The scoped obstruction is derived jointly with the dynamics.

## 1. Claimed scoped conclusion and the unchanged benchmark

For every integer m>=1536 fix the SAME enclosing parameters

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)),
    a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j, b_j=1+d_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, n=2^m-1,
    H_base=1/(1+2S_m).                                      (1.1)

All children use this enclosing m. Let alpha_plus,alpha_minus belong to
[0,1], with arbitrary dependence on m and the complete word, and put

    f(x)=alpha_plus*x^2/2  (x>=0),
         alpha_minus*x^2/2 (x<=0).                         (1.2)

For any initial point of positive closest distance to argmin f, use the
ORIGINAL physical updates x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t*f'(x_t), with h=H_m, and set

    V=2f(x_n)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.                         (1.3)

The joint argument below claims, uniformly over both curvatures and both
legitimate initial signs,

    V <= H_base/2,                 m>=1536.                (1.4)

The constants and threshold1536 are independent of the curvature choices.
The same bound therefore holds for their supremum. This is a family upper
bound, NOT an upper bound for the unrestricted W_n(H_m).

Keep the exact original benchmark definitions

    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_N=max_(0<=j<N) Omega(C_j,C_(N-j-1)),
    B_N=max_(0<=j<N) G(C_j,B_(N-j-1)), U_N^ZJ=1/B_N.        (1.5)

These retain every integer split and are only definitions, not executed
recurrences. Accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7, (36)--(38),(77), supplies

    2<c<121/50, 1/4<a<21/50,
    M_j=v*c^j+(1-v)*2^j<=c^j, v=a/(c-2) in(0,1),
    B_n*H_base<237/250.                                    (1.6)

Only these hierarchy/benchmark parts of that fact are used. No spiral
construction conclusion is imported. Consequently (1.4), if verified, gives

    B_n*V <= B_n*H_base/2 <237/500.                         (1.7)

Accepted b98bb807d514e80d and1fca3e5dd555cb4a give

    H_base <= K_n(H_m) <= F_ray_n(H_m) <= W_n(H_m).          (1.8)

Here K_n is the maximum of the global Huber term and EVERY single-cut term;
F_ray_n is the full feasible alternating-ray maximum, not a chosen cut.
Thus adjoining this entire two-curvature family to either complete channel
does not improve that channel on any of these horizons. No new formula for
the full ray maximum is asserted or needed.

The original question asks about all sufficiently large horizons and every
nonnegative word, or a full-class worst-case upper certificate for a negative
answer. Neither conclusion follows from (1.4). We keep W=2R throughout.

## 2. One global function, all minima, actual normalization and all rows

The single derivative is alpha_plus*x on the positive ray and alpha_minus*x
on the negative ray, with value0 at0. It is continuous and nondecreasing.
On each ray it is 1-Lipschitz. Across0, for u,v>=0,

    |f'(u)-f'(-v)|=alpha_plus*u+alpha_minus*v<=u+v.

Hence (1.2) is globally convex, C1 and 1-smooth. Its exact minimum is0.
If both curvatures are positive its complete minimum set is{0}. If just one
curvature vanishes, its minimum set is the whole closed ray of zero curvature.
If both vanish it is all of R and there is no initial point of positive
closest distance. This last function contributes no admissible instance.

When the minimum set is a ray, a legitimate initial point lies strictly on
the other ray, its closest minimizer is0, and its distance is |x_0|. A later
entry into the flat ray is a minimizer and all subsequent updates are fixed.
Starting on the flat ray is excluded from (1.3); it is not assigned a fictitious
positive closest radius. For positive initial distance, positive homogeneity
allows x_0=+1 or-1 without changing (1.3). Its true distance is then1.

Advertised smoothness is1 regardless of max(alpha_plus,alpha_minus).
For advertised L>0 use L*f and h_t/L; this produces exactly the same points
and payoff. No smaller actual smoothness is substituted while holding h fixed.

All physical steps, for an arbitrary nonnegative finite word, are exactly

    x_(t+1)=(1-h_t*alpha_sign(x_t))*x_t, x_t!=0;
    x_(t+1)=0, x_t=0.                                     (2.1)

The multiplier is positive/preserving for h_t*alpha<1, zero/absorbing at
equality, and negative/crossing for h_t*alpha>1. A zero step is the identity;
zero curvature is the identity on its entire ray; arbitrary large or unordered
steps require no exception. Repeated queries always use exactly the same
derivative and objective. Knot0 and both slopes were fixed before the run.
The integral on a ray to radius u is exactly alpha*u^2/2; no hidden area is
discarded. No spline completion or radius-changing modification is used.

For every actual sample, including the unqueried terminal and every minimizer,
write g_i=f'(x_i), f_i=f(x_i). Use the convention of fact1252b8823bb5a290:

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2.              (2.2)

For two points on the same ray of curvature A,

    Q_ij=-A*(1-A)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0.                       (2.3)

For opposite rays, let x_i=sigma*u, x_j=-sigma*v, u,v>=0,
and let A,B be their respective fixed curvatures. Expansion gives

    Q_ij=-A*(1-A)*u^2/2-B*(1-B)*v^2/2-A*(1-B)*u*v<=0.
                                                                    (2.4)

Reversing i,j replaces the last coefficient by B*(1-A), again nonnegative
before its minus sign. These formulas include all remote pairs, both directed
optimum rows, ties, zero curvatures and arbitrary minimizers on a flat ray.
The actual full Gram matrix is z*z^T for z=(x_0,g_0,...,g_n), so is PSD.
Thus this is an explicit globally interpolating function, not merely a list
of local updates whose remote rows are left unchecked.

## 3. Exact two-state composition, including equalities

Reflect the line if necessary and label the two rays F and S by their
curvatures p>=q>=0. F denotes the larger-curvature ray; labels are arbitrary
when p=q. For a nonzero state its data are(sign label, positive magnitude).
Add an absorbing zero state. At level j define T_j by

    (u,A) -> (u,(1-u*b_j)A) if u*b_j<1;
    (u,A) -> zero             if u*b_j=1;
    (u,A) -> (other,(u*b_j-1)A) if u*b_j>1,               (3.1)

where u is p or q according to the current ray; the ray label is retained
separately even when p=q. Zero maps to zero. If Map_j is the complete
state-and-magnitude map for H_j, then

    Map_0=identity, Map_(j+1)=Map_j composed with T_j composed with Map_j. (3.2)

The rightmost map acts first. This exactly composes every original physical
step; it does not replace a block by its mass. In particular H_m ends with
the original level0 leaf b_0=1+a. The same formula (3.1) at that leaf applies
to its ACTUAL preterminal sign, including equality and an already zero state.

We now replace this recursion by closed products. Define

    P_j(t)=product_(ell=0)^(j-1)
                      (1-t*b_ell)^(2^(j-ell-1)), P_0(t)=1,
    r=min({j in[0,m-1]:p*b_j>=1} union{m}),
    s=min({j in[0,m-1]:q*b_j>=1} union{m}).               (3.3)

These are weak crossing thresholds: equality is explicitly included.
Since b_j strictly increases, r<=s. All factors in P_r(p) and P_s(q)
are strictly positive. For any curvature u whose first weak threshold is r<m put

    eta_r(u)=P_r(u)*(u*b_r-1)>=0.                         (3.4)

For a unit initial radius, A_m(F),A_m(S) below are the terminal magnitudes.
The payoff is its squared magnitude times the terminal curvature.

CASE I: r=s=m. There are no crossings or threshold hits at any query:

    A_m(F)=P_m(p), A_m(S)=P_m(q),
    V_F=p*P_m(p)^2, V_S=q*P_m(q)^2.                     (3.5)

Exclude the flat initial sign when its curvature is0, as in Section2.

CASE II: r=s<m. For either starting ray with curvature u, let v be the other
curvature, and put N=2^(m-r-1). Then

    A_m(u)=[eta_r(u)*P_r(v)]^N
                   *product_(j=r+1)^(m-1)(v*b_j-1)^(2^(m-j-1)),
    V_u=v*A_m(u)^2.                                     (3.6)

A nonzero terminal is on the other ray. A zero expression denotes absorption,
not a prescribed nonzero sign. Thus (3.6) remains exact if one curvature is
exactly1/b_r and the other is larger: it does NOT incorrectly declare both
starting signs killed. Only the starting ray whose eta factor is zero dies.

To prove (3.6), all H_r subwords preserve each sign and multiply it by P_r
at that curvature. Contract the notation to the ORIGINAL H_r subwords and
the intervening checkpoints of levels>=r. There are2^(m-r) such subwords
and2^(m-r)-1 checkpoints. Provided the trajectory has not hit zero, the
checkpoints all cross. The level-r checkpoints occupy even zero-based
checkpoint indices, hence all take the starting curvature u. Every higher
checkpoint occupies an odd index and takes v. Half the H_r subwords take
each curvature. A level j occurs2^(m-j-1) times. Multiplying precisely these
factors yields (3.6). If an equality is encountered, its zero factor gives
the same absorbed result; alternatively continuity gives that endpoint.

CASE III: r<s. Define the strictly positive denominator and fixed ratio

    D=P_r(q)*(1-q*b_r), R=eta_r(p)/D>=0,
    K=0 if s=m, and K=2^(m-s)-1 if s<m.                  (3.7)

Then the exact full terminal formulas are

    A_m(S)=|P_m(q)|*R^K,
    A_m(F)=|P_m(q)|*R^(K+1),
    V_S=q*P_m(q)^2*R^(2K),
    V_F=q*P_m(q)^2*R^(2K+2).                            (3.8)

Use R^0=1, also when R=0. Nonzero terminals are on the q ray. If q=0,
the F-start formula can give positive terminal magnitude on the flat ray,
but its objective is exactly0. This does not turn it into a non-minimizer.

For a proof, H_(r+1) sends every surviving starting sign to S, with gains

    A_(r+1)(S)=P_r(q)^2*(1-q*b_r),
    A_(r+1)(F)=eta_r(p)*P_r(q)=R*A_(r+1)(S).             (3.9)

The report-local R in (3.7) is a gain ratio, distinct from the original
performance normalization R=W/2. If Map_j has the constant surviving output
and gain ratio R, applying (3.2)
preserves the same gain ratio. Its S-input gain is squared and multiplied
by |1-q*b_j| if that checkpoint preserves the q sign, and by
R*|1-q*b_j| if it crosses. At equality the whole map is zero. Up to the
first q threshold, its S gain is P_j(q). For all later levels, multiplication
of the factors R with their exact tree multiplicities gives total exponent
sum_(j=s)^(m-1)2^(m-j-1)=2^(m-s)-1. This proves (3.8).
At an equality q*b_s=1, its P_m(q) factor vanishes, so both starts die.
If p*b_r=1, R=0: the F start dies immediately, and the S start dies on its
first excursion to F if one occurs. These cases agree with (3.1).

Equations (3.5),(3.6),(3.8) are exact finite terminal products for ALL curvature
pairs and ALL m in (1.1), not just fixed-parameter limits or a numerical run.
The state map (3.1) also gives every intermediate query and objective:
x_t=sign_t*A_t, g_t=alpha_sign_t*x_t, f_t=alpha_sign_t*A_t^2/2.
Thus it fixes the full actual history and Gram as well as its terminal.

For clarity about the final leaf, if Case II has r>=1, that leaf preserves
the terminal ray v and is already counted inside P_r(v). If r=0 it crosses
from the initial curvature u to v and is included among the level0 factors.
In Case III with r>=1 the final leaf preserves S. If r=0, s>=2 it also
preserves S; if r=0,s=1 a surviving penultimate level1 checkpoint crosses
S to F, and the final leaf crosses F to S. Equality at that penultimate
checkpoint kills the state. All these are physical updates, not truncations
before the leaf. Zero states stay zero in every case.

## 4. Uniform elementary estimates used in the joint proof

We derive every additional bound here rather than importing an unverified
draft. From m>=1536, beta>99/100 and (1.6),

    2/5<a<21/50<1/2, 2<c<5/2, a*c=beta<1,
    c*(c-1)<(121/50)*(71/50)<7/2.                       (4.1)

For example a>(99/100)/(121/50)=99/242>2/5. Also S_1=1+a>d_1=beta,
and the original recurrence gives

    S_(j+1)-d_(j+1)=2(S_j-d_j)+1+(3-c)*d_j>0.

Consequently S_j>d_j for j>=1. Since m>=3, S_(m-1)>3 and

    1+2S_m<2c^m,
    1+2S_m=4S_(m-1)+2b_(m-1)+1<7S_(m-1).              (4.2)

The first inequality uses M_m<=c^m; the second uses b_(m-1)<S_(m-1)+1.

For every j>=2 we will use

    S_j/b_j>7/5,             (S_j-b_0)/b_j>9/10.        (4.3)

At j=2, S_2=3+2a+beta>479/100 and b_2=1+beta*c<171/50, so
S_2/b_2>479/342>7/5 (2395>2394). Also
S_2-b_0=2+a+beta>339/100, so (S_2-b_0)/b_2>339/342>9/10.
For induction b_(j+1)=c*b_j-(c-1)<c*b_j. If S_j>t*b_j then
S_(j+1)>(2t+1)b_j>t*c*b_j for t=7/5, since c<5/2<2+1/t.
If S_j-b_0>t*b_j then S_(j+1)-b_0>(2t+1)b_j+b_0>t*c*b_j
for t=9/10 by the same comparison. This proves both bounds for every j>=2;
no false assertion of monotonicity of S_j/b_j is needed.

If r>=1 and u has first weak threshold r, then
u*b_(r-1)<1<=u*b_r and

    0<=eta_r(u)
       <=(1-u*b_(r-1))*(u*b_r-1)
       <=(b_r-b_(r-1))^2/(4*b_r*b_(r-1))
       <(c-1)^2/(4c)<1/4.                              (4.4)

Indeed all omitted factors of P_r(u) lie in(0,1]; the middle quadratic
has the displayed maximum by completing its square. With t=b_r/b_(r-1),
1<t<c and (t-1)^2/t=t-2+1/t increases for t>1. Finally (c-1)^2<c for
2<c<5/2. If r=0, eta_0(u)=u*b_0-1<=a. Hence in ALL cases

    eta_r(u)<=a; for r>=1 it is <1/4.                   (4.5)

For any pre-threshold scalar block, all its factors belong to(0,1], and
1-z<=exp(-z) for z>=0 gives

    P_j(t)<=exp(-t*S_j).                               (4.6)

Whenever r<s, P_s(q)/D is a product with nonnegative integer exponents
of pre-threshold factors, so it is at most1. It retains a factor
1-q*b_(s-1) when s>=r+2. When s=r+1 it equals P_r(q).
Deleting the factors of D deletes exactly the mass S_r+b_r from P_s.
In particular for r=0 it gives

    P_s(q)/D<=exp(-q*(S_s-b_0)).                        (4.7)

For later use, exp(7/5)>4: its Taylor terms of orders0 through5 already
sum to more than4. Also exp(9/10)>2 by its first three terms, and e>5/2
by its first four terms. These are inequalities between exact series terms,
not floating-point estimates or evaluated mathematical programs.

## 5. Product envelope for all high-level factors

This is an algebraic estimate inside the same joint proof. Suppose a
nonnegative sequence of complete block gains has initial value J at level t
and for subsequent levels is squared and multiplied by factors at most
z*c^ell, ell=0,...,k-1. Expanding the finite product, its final value is at most

    J^(2^k)*z^(2^k-1)*c^(2^k-k-1)
        =J*c^(-k)*(c*z*J)^(2^k-1),        k>=0.          (5.1)

For k=0 the expression is J. The exponent identity follows by induction:
the weighted sum of ell over ell=0,...,k-1 is2^k-k-1. Formula (5.1) involves
no division by J or z and remains valid when either vanishes (0^0=1 only in
the empty-product cases). It is an upper bound for actual products, never
an independently asserted transfer factor.

## 6. Case III with a q threshold: uniformly priced returns

Assume r<s<m. If a threshold equality already gives absorption, the desired
upper bound follows directly. The following formulas also include those
zeros. Set k=m-s>=1, and define

    J_s=P_s(q)*max(1,R),
    E_s=c*R*q*d_s*P_s(q).                               (6.1)

At levels j>=s, 0<=q*b_j-1=q*d_j-(1-q)<=q*d_j. The exact gain recursion
used to prove (3.8), followed by (5.1) with z=R*q*d_s, gives

    max(A_m(F),A_m(S))
        <=J_s*c^(-k)*E_s^(2^k-1).                      (6.2)

Here R is fixed by the two curvatures and their ORIGINAL lower block; it is
not re-chosen at a return. We now price BOTH factors J_s and E_s uniformly.

First,

    E_s<4/5                                             (6.3)

for every r<s. If s>=r+2, put y=q*b_(s-1) in[0,1). By (4.5) and the retained
factor noted after (4.6),

    E_s=c*q*d_s*eta_r(p)*P_s(q)/D
       <=c^2*eta_r(p)*y*(1-y)
       <=c^2*a/4=c*beta/4<5/8<4/5.                     (6.4)

If s=r+1 and r>=1, the ratio P_s/D=P_r(q) retains1-q*b_(r-1). Put
y=q*b_(r-1) in[0,1). Since q*d_s<=c^2*y, (4.4) gives

    E_s<=c^3*eta_r(p)*y*(1-y)
       <=c^2*(c-1)^2/16<49/64<4/5.                    (6.5)

If r=0,s=1, then E_s=c*q*d_1*eta_0(p)<=beta^2*q.
Here q<1/b_0<5/7<4/5, proving (6.3) in the remaining case.

Second, for s>=2,

    J_s<1/4.                                           (6.6)

Indeed q*b_s>=1, so (4.3),(4.6) give P_s(q)<=exp(-S_s/b_s)<exp(-7/5)<1/4.
If r>=1 then R*P_s(q)=eta_r(p)*P_s(q)/D<1/4 by (4.4) and the quotient
bound1. If r=0, (4.3),(4.7) give
R*P_s(q)<=a*exp(-9/10)<a/2<1/4. This proves (6.6).

For s=1,r=0 we instead have J_1=max(1-q*b_0,eta_0(p))<1/2: since
q>=1/b_1, b_1=1+beta<2 and b_0>7/5, its first entry is less than3/10,
and its second is at most a<1/2.

The true terminal objective is q times the squared magnitude. Since
q*b_(s-1)<1, we have q<c/d_s. For s>=2, (4.2),(6.2),(6.3),(6.6) give

    (1+2S_m)*V
      <[c/(8a)]*c^(-k)*(4/5)^(2*(2^k-1))
      <=[1/(8a)]*(16/25)<1/5<1/2.                     (6.7)

The middle expression is decreasing with integer k>=1 because c>1 and
4/5<1; at k=1 it has the stated value. We used a>2/5 in the final inequality.

For s=1 the same calculation using J_1<1/2 gives

    (1+2S_m)*V
      <[c/(2a)]*c^(-k)*(4/5)^(2*(2^k-1)).              (6.8)

Now k=m-1>=2. Its value is at most
(4/5)^6/(2*a*c)=(4/5)^6/(2*beta)<(2/3)*(4/5)^6<1/2.
Thus ALL separated-threshold pairs with a q crossing are covered, including
arbitrary m-dependent r,s,p,q. No bounded-away-from-zero condition on q was used.

## 7. Case II: synchronized thresholds, including both high curvatures

First let r=s=0. Every surviving step crosses; its magnitude multiplier is
at most b_j-1=d_j since both curvatures are at most1. Thus

    A_m(u)<=product_(j=0)^(m-1)d_j^(2^(m-j-1))
           =beta^(2^m-1)*c^(-m).                       (7.1)

The equality follows because the total exponent of a is2^m-1 and that of c
is2^m-m-1, with a*c=beta. Terminal curvature is at most1, hence

    (1+2S_m)*V<2*c^(-m)*beta^(2*(2^m-1))<1/2           (7.2)

for m>=3, which includes every claimed horizon. Threshold hits only reduce
the product to0.

Next let 1<=r=s<m and fix either initial curvature u, other curvature v.
Put t=r+1, k=m-t, J=eta_r(u)*P_r(v), and E=c*v*d_t*J. By (4.4),

    J<1/4.                                             (7.3)

The same y=v*b_(r-1) in[0,1) calculation as (6.5) gives

    E<=c^3*eta_r(u)*y*(1-y)
      <=c^2*(c-1)^2/16<49/64<4/5.                     (7.4)

Notice that v here may be either the larger or smaller curvature. Both lie
in the same threshold interval, which is all this estimate requires.
Using the EXACT product (3.6), all its higher factors are at most v*d_j,
so (5.1) gives A_m(u)<=J*c^(-k)*E^(2^k-1).
Also v<1/b_(r-1)<c^2/d_t. If k>=1, (4.2) yields

    (1+2S_m)*V_u
      <[c^2/(8a)]*c^(-k)*(4/5)^(2*(2^k-1))
      <=[c/(8a)]*(16/25)=2c/(25a)<1/2.                 (7.5)

The last strict bound uses c<5/2 and a>2/5, so its upper endpoint is1/2.
This is uniform even when r and both curvatures depend arbitrarily on m.

If k=0, only the highest-level checkpoint crosses: r=m-1 and the exact
terminal is J. By (4.4),(4.6),

    V_u=v*eta_r(u)^2*P_r(v)^2
        <=(1/16)*v*exp(-2v*S_(m-1))
        <=1/[32e*S_(m-1)].                            (7.6)

The elementary maximum of z*exp(-2Az), z>=0, is1/(2eA) for A>0,
as follows by differentiating; no numerical maximizer is used.
Equation (4.2) makes (1+2S_m)*V_u<7/(32e)<1/2. This completes Case II.

## 8. No q crossing, small curvatures, and all boundary cases

If r=s=m, (3.5),(4.6) and the same exponential maximum give, for either
legitimate initial curvature u,

    V_u<=1/(2e*S_m),
    (1+2S_m)*V_u<7/(6e)<7/15<1/2,                    (8.1)

where S_m>3 and e>5/2 were used. In particular arbitrarily small,
horizon-dependent curvatures are included.

If r<s=m, a start on S has this same bound. For a start on F, (3.8) reads
A_m(F)=eta_r(p)*P_m(q)/D. The quotient deletes from the original product
exactly one copy of H_r and its following b_r, retaining only factors in(0,1].
Since S_r+b_r<=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1), the remaining mass is at least S_(m-1).
Consequently (4.5),(4.6) imply

    A_m(F)<=a*exp(-q*S_(m-1)),
    V_F<=a^2/(2e*S_(m-1)),
    (1+2S_m)*V_F<7a^2/(2e)<7/(8e)<1/2.                (8.2)

For q=0 the actual terminal on S has zero objective, so (8.2) is still valid.
If there is no crossing from the positive-curvature ray, its formula is
instead (3.5) and it remains covered by (8.1). This addresses the entire
nontrivial zero-curvature boundary with its own true closest radius.

The weak-threshold definitions and zero factors in Section3 handle all
equalities explicitly. Alternatively, for each fixed finite word and initial
sign the map

    (p,q,x) -> x-h*(p*x if x is on F, q*x if x is on S)

is jointly continuous, including x=0, because both branches vanish there.
Induction gives continuity of the full terminal point and its objective in
both curvatures. On strictly positive curvatures its closest radius from a
unit start is1. At an admissible zero-curvature limit the start remains on
the positive-curvature side and that distance remains1. Thus the uniform
bound also extends to every admissible threshold/axis endpoint. This is a
finite-horizon continuity statement, not an interchange of a limit with a
supremum. Since ALL domains above share the SAME numerical bound1/2 and
the SAME threshold1536, arbitrary parameter choices depending on m obey (1.4).

## 9. Counterexample screen and exact scope of the limitation

The direct-proving plan first obtains the complete products, then prices the
gain ratio. The tempting shortcut R<=1 is FALSE, so it is not used. For an
exact all-word example choose first physical level b_0=3/2, p=1 and q=3/5.
At that level the F-input magnitude becomes1/2 on S while the S-input
magnitude becomes1/10 on S; hence the ratio is5. Both use the same static
quadratic with curvatures1 and3/5. This tests the shortcut only; it is not
the hierarchy (1.1). The actual proof bounds the combined quantities
J_s=P_s(q)*max(1,R) and E_s, which retain the contraction that a bare R loses.

A second fragile inference is that equality of q*b_r with1 kills BOTH
initial signs in Case II. It need not: at the first H_(r+1), a strictly
crossing u start uses u, not q, at the checkpoint and lands on q only after
it. Formula (3.6) gives eta_r(u)*P_r(q)>0 in that case, while a q start dies.
The state machine and final-leaf checks deliberately preserve this distinction.

Overlapping scalar returns are legal and are not eliminated by our hypotheses.
For example, on f(x)=x^2/4 with physical word(1,6,1,6,1), (2.1) gives
1,1/2,-1,-1/2,1,1/2. The same positive interval is revisited and the same
derivative x/2 is used. This is a simple explanatory example already known
from the previous round; its computation here is a written finite identity,
not a trajectory program, and it is not used to prove the hierarchy bound.

Our limitation rests on homogeneity on EACH ray: a ray's curvature does not
vary with radius, so every H_j is a two-state homogeneous map. Once its two
inputs have the same output ray, their gain ratio freezes. A spatially shifted
or multi-knot fixed derivative can lose both properties; no bound for that
larger class follows. Similarly, this does not merely reapply the prior
disjoint-inward argument: no radial ordering of repeated visits appears in
Sections3--8, and every large step inside every child is retained.

This law is globally legitimate on every nonnegative word by Sections2--3.
No fallback is needed for its feasibility. A positive general-word witness
exists within it by choosing p=q=1/[2*(1+sum h)], starting from1: all
multipliers are positive and the objective is positive. This elementary
existence observation supplies no exact-profile lower strength. On the
diagnostic hierarchy, the ENTIRE family is below H_base/2 and therefore
cannot improve the complete K or ray channels, even if selected using the
full word. The original all-word comparison remains a theorem-level task.

At most one second law was permitted, not required. No shifted-curvature
or spline law is asserted here. The useful outcome of this invocation is
the uniform two-curvature screen and its exact products, preserved with
their UNVERIFIED status rather than extrapolated beyond their scope.

## 10. Recall, literature, skills, and resources

The relevant shared state and assignment guidance efacaf1e71778e76 were read.
Final readback found TASK unchanged and later guidance29dd9d75f3f7d055
explicitly retaining the max3r73 assignment and cap.
Prior finding13c7d6e297580087 was recalled solely to identify its disjoint
inward hypothesis; none of its new arguments is an accepted brick here.
Accepted facts were searched first. Relevant statements/proofs read were
1252b8823bb5a290 (normalization and ordered residual convention),
b98bb807d514e80d (complete K),1fca3e5dd555cb4a (complete ray construction),
and5fd396eba75651e7 (hierarchy constants and exact benchmark scope).
The shared glossary was checked; local reused symbols are defined in this
report and in the accompanying glossary. No other worker's private files
were read. The per-worker cap overrides general multi-agent instructions.

Skills: query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving,
construct-counterexamples. The active search program was repair of the
sign-dependent full-history bound, at active_program stage. Matlas returned
three unrelated/insufficient results: nonconvex stochastic ADMM, a
convex-concave extragradient inequality, and self-contracted gradient descent
with steps bounded by1/L. The last assumption explicitly fails for the
present original word. None was imported. Primary-source web search found
the Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur interpolation paper, arXiv1502.05666. Its exact
local text downloads/taylor-hendrickx-glineur-1502.05666.txt was read in
Section2.4 and both proof directions of Lemma2. This supplies background on
global interpolation; our two-ray residual calculation instead proves every
row directly. No new external theorem is a proof premise. The exact
two-state mechanism has reduced to finite elementary products, so no extra
literature theorem or unexamined paper is needed to price it.

Tool work was source retrieval and administrative text/file operations.
All mathematics above is written reasoning. Mathematical programs0;
numerical/arithmetic replay0; executed trajectories/recurrences0;
optimizer/solver/benchmark DP0; formal calls/fact_submit0; new facts0;
installations0; paid consults0; subagents0. No old allowance was reopened.
No outward publication, finalization, fact revocation, shutdown, or Goal

This completes the one authorized analytical invocation only. Its outer
controller's post-return count is not observable before return. There is
