# Both actual spiral families: all parameters and all checkpoint counts

Status: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical draft, with the precise scope
stated in Section 12. Worker max2, one allocated round55, plan
65b767be26cbd9d8, master_guidance5c4d3ccc1ebe4c55. No formal gate is
authorized. No assertion in this file is a newly accepted fact.

Both the source realization and the main realization are treated for
every allowed parameter pair and every eligible ordered selection,
without a checkpoint-count restriction. Section 9A supplies the new
DISCRETE low-turn estimate for the main band C<c(c-2). All other
components are included and proved in this same joint manuscript.

The complete recalled findings e65119d6d7a877a9,172014083da57ddf,
59d88015afb74486,717b541e05f4484f are provenance and warnings, not proof
premises. Their reusable arguments are written out again below; no
unverified intermediate is adopted as an accepted fact. Every geometric
and comparison ingredient needed here is proved jointly. Accepted
fact04219554e41d9750 is used only for the
original benchmark and its saved enclosure in Section 11. Accepted
fact5b7ff4253c38b90a concerns the original tau=1 family and is not applied
to the small-tau words considered here.

## 1. Physical words, parameters, and conventions

All norms and inner products are Euclidean. A subscripted e_i denotes
the standard coordinate vector in the dimension of its occurrence;
the explicitly defined planar e(theta) below is a separate notation.
For a finite nonnegative
word h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), the physical iteration at advertised smoothness
one is x_(t+1)=x_t-h_t grad f(x_t). Define its actual closest-distance
payoff by

    E(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.

The construction always has positive initial distance. Its advertised
smoothness remains one; it is not retuned to a smaller Lipschitz constant.
Multiplying f by any L>0 and using h_t/L preserves all points and E.
W_n(h) is the supremum over the original full class of such instances;
an upper bound for the constructed family is not an upper bound on W_n.

Write a selection of k checkpoints in original physical order as

    h=(block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),

where s_i=sum block_i. An ordinary block may be empty, consist of zeros,
or contain arbitrarily large steps. One pair of independent parameters
is fixed for the entire selection:

    kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2,
    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    C=chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2) exp(kappa theta_sp),
    T=(exp((1-kappa^2)theta_sp/kappa)-1)/(1-kappa^2),  kappa<1,
    T=theta_sp/kappa,                                kappa>=1,
    H_sp=C[1+(1+kappa)T]-1,
    A=A_sp=epsilon+(1+H_sp)/epsilon.                     (1)

The two branches for T agree continuously at kappa=1. C is an arc
constant, not a benchmark denominator C_n; A is an activation allowance,
not a primitive-certificate capacity. In particular C>1 and A>=2C.

The source realization requires

    b_i>=2(1+epsilon^(-2)) for every selected i.          (2)

For the main realization define

    qmax=1,                                         kappa<=epsilon,
    qmax=sqrt((1+epsilon^2)/(1+kappa^2))
         exp(kappa[theta_sp-arctan(1/kappa)]),        kappa>epsilon,
    N_sp=max(0,kappa qmax-epsilon),
    B_sp=1+max(epsilon^(-2),N_sp/epsilon),

and require b_i>=B_sp. All thresholds include equality, and the kappa>1
normal correction is retained.

The physical hierarchy is fixed by an enclosing integer m>=1536 and
real 0<tau<=1/4:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    H_(m,tau)=H_m, S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1.              (3)

Every child uses this same enclosing(m,tau). No physical entry is
removed or reordered. Let H_base(h)=1/(1+2 sum h).

## 2. Actual backward blocks, including zeros and caps

For 0<=theta<=theta_sp put

    R(theta)=C exp(-kappa theta),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)),
    n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa t(theta),
    K=conv({0} union {v(theta):0<=theta<=theta_sp}).

The endpoints are (C,0) and (epsilon,-1). With Delta=phi-theta,

    <n(theta),v(phi)>/R(theta)
       =exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+kappa sin Delta).

Its derivative in Delta is
-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta. Since |Delta|<pi/2,
its maximum is one at zero. The value at the added point0 is zero.
Thus n(theta) supports K at v(theta); (0,1) also supports K at(C,0).
Differentiation gives

    p'=-R(kappa cos theta+sin theta)<0,
    q'=R(cos theta-kappa sin theta),
    p>=epsilon, q>=0, p>=epsilon q.                    (4)

The sign of q' proves the qmax formula in Section 1.

For an actual local chronological list alpha_1,...,alpha_r, alpha_j>=0,
set r_r=v_r=(epsilon,-1). Given r_j=(u_j,-y_j) and its projected arc
point v_j=v(theta_j), use alpha=alpha_j. If

    y_j<=kappa[u_j+(alpha-1)C],                          (5)

set v_(j-1)=(C,0), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha(C,0). Otherwise choose the unique
theta in(0,theta_j] solving

    <r_j,e(theta)-kappa^(-1)t(theta)>/R(theta)=1-alpha,  (6)

and set v_(j-1)=v(theta), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha v(theta).
These definitions specify real numbers; no root search is executed.

Here is the existence and projection proof. Inductively
u_j>=epsilon, y_j>=1, y_j/u_j<=1/epsilon. Before a cap the stronger
representation is

    r_j=R(theta_j)[(1+nu_j)e(theta_j)+kappa nu_j t(theta_j)],
    nu_j>=0.                                           (7)

It holds at the endpoint with nu_r=0. If(5) holds, put
mu=u_j+(alpha-1)C>=y_j/kappa>0. Then

    r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu n(0)+(0,kappa mu-y_j),

a nonnegative combination of supporting normals. Projection follows.
The same formula gives y_(j-1)<=kappa(u_(j-1)-C), so every earlier
backward step is capped, including zero steps.

If(5) fails, the current state is uncapped. The left side of(6) is one
at theta_j by(7), is less than1-alpha at zero, and has derivative
(kappa+kappa^(-1))(u_j cos theta+y_j sin theta)/R(theta)>0. There is
exactly one root, including theta=theta_j when alpha=0. Representation
(7) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least theta_j. Consequently
<r_j,t(theta)>=0 at the root. Equation(6) makes
r_j+(alpha-1)v(theta) a nonnegative multiple of n(theta), proving the
preceding projection and(7). Adding alpha(p,-q), using(4), preserves
the stated bounds on u_j,y_j and their ratio.

Define the actual outputs

    P=sum_j alpha_j p(theta_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_j alpha_j q(theta_(j-1)), s_loc=sum_j alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), P>=epsilon Q, P<=Cs_loc.
For consecutive uncapped angles theta_old<=theta_new let
Delta=theta_new-theta_old. Resolving the backward equation along
t(theta_old) gives the exact scalar relation

    nu_old=B_D+A_D nu_new,
    A_D=exp(-kappa Delta)(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_D=exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta/kappa.                (8)

Both coefficients are nonnegative. For d=1-kappa^2,
A_D'-(d/kappa)A_D=-(1+kappa^(-2))exp(-kappa Delta)sin Delta<=0.
If kappa<1, then A_D<=exp(d Delta/kappa), B_D<=Delta/kappa, and
1+dB_D<=exp(d Delta/kappa). Telescoping
1+d nu_old<=exp(d Delta/kappa)(1+d nu_new) proves nu_j<=T.
If kappa>=1, A_D<=1 and B_D<=Delta/kappa give nu_j<=T directly by
summing angle differences. Therefore every uncapped state has
y_j<=C[1+(1+kappa)T]. A cap leaves y unchanged. We have proved

    0<=Q<=H_sp, P>=epsilon Q, 0<=P<=Cs_loc.             (9)

Two lower bounds from the actual slopes will supply UPPER payoff bounds:

    p(theta)+q(theta)>=1, P+Q>=s_loc,
    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2 >=(1+epsilon+s_loc)^2/2.       (10)

Indeed R(theta)>=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)>=1 and cos theta+sin theta>=1.
The quadratic inequality is (x+y)^2<=2(x^2+y^2). It is an inequality
for actual chronological outputs, not a claim they depend only on s_loc.

For the main assembly we also need y_j>=q_j and u_j-p_j>=-N_sp.
The first follows from(7), since
y_j-q_j=R nu_j(sin theta_j+kappa cos theta_j)>=0; at a cap q_j=0.
A nonnegative horizontal normal gives u_j>=p_j. Otherwise
theta_j>arctan(1/kappa), so p_j<=kappa qmax while u_j>=epsilon.
At a cap u_j>=C. This proves both estimates for every kappa>0.

## 3. Finite local functions and their exact zero wedges

Take a local checkpoint b_loc>=1+epsilon^(-2), and a positive sigma
satisfying

    sigma(P+epsilon b_loc)<=1,
    sigma[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.                  (11)

Let

    z_circ=(1-sigma(epsilon+P),sigma(1+Q))=(c_1,c_2),
    U=1-sigma(P+epsilon b_loc), Y=sigma Q,
    V={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_r},
    phi(z)=sigma max_(v in V)<v,z-z_circ>,
    f_loc(x)=min_z[phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].                 (12)

Every plane remains in V, including both endpoints and every actual
projected slope. Since
(p,-q)=[(p-epsilon q)/C](C,0)+q(epsilon,-1), its coefficients are
nonnegative. Thus the exact zero set of phi is

    Z_phi={z:z_1<=c_1, epsilon(z_1-c_1)-(z_2-c_2)<=0}.  (13)

The two endpoint planes prove necessity and the conic representation
proves sufficiency. Formula(11) gives phi=0 on z_1<=0,z_2>=0.
Also <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma[(1-b_loc)epsilon p+q]<=0, so
phi(U,y)=0 for all y>=Y. In particular U>=0.

For clarity, the envelope properties used here have elementary proofs.
A nonnegative finite affine maximum J having a zero has a unique
proximal minimizer z at each x, by coercivity and strict convexity of
J(z)+||x-z||^2/2. Put g=x-z; its optimality condition is g in partial J(z).
For another pair z',g', subgradient monotonicity gives
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. Using z as a trial point gives
env_1 J(x+w)<=env_1 J(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2; if z' is the new proximal
point, the subgradient at z gives
env_1 J(x+w)>=env_1 J(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2. These prove convexity,
differentiability, gradient g, and 1-Lipschitz gradient. Attainment and
nonnegativity prove argmin(env_1 J)={J=0}, with minimum zero.

At x_j=z_circ+sigma r_j the point x_j-sigma v_j is proximal: the
projection inequality against all v in V makes sigma v_j an active
subgradient of phi there. The actual local gradient is sigma v_j.
Consequently the local path starts at(1,0), follows every alpha_j,
reaches(1-sigma P,sigma Q), and the local checkpoint b_loc sends it to
(U,Y+sigma b_loc). Throughout the ordinary block its first coordinate
is at least U and its second belongs to[0,Y].

## 4. Source realization: one static function and every physical step

For every i<k apply Section 2 to block_i with each physical step divided
by2. In Sections4--8, P_i,Q_i mean precisely these HALF-step outputs.
Use the local checkpoint b_loc=b_i/2 and the unchanged scale

    sigma'_i=1/[Cs_i/2+epsilon b_i/2+A].                (14)

Equations(9),(1) prove both conditions(11). Define primed local objects
phi'_i,c'_(i,1),c'_(i,2),U'_i,Y'_i from(12) and let f_i=env_1 phi'_i.
Set

    G_i=sigma'_i b_i/2=b_i/(Cs_i+2A+epsilon b_i),
    D'_0=1, ell'_0=0,
    D'_(i+1)=D'_i G_i, ell'_(i+1)=D'_i sigma'_i Q_i,
    delta'=D'_k/(1+s_k).                               (15)

Define Hub_d(z) to be0 for z<=0, z^2/2 for0<=z<=d, and
dz-d^2/2 for z>=d. Its derivative is respectively0,z,d, a continuous
nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz function. On R^(k+1), from x_0=e_0, use exactly

    F(x)=(1/2)sum_(i<k)(D'_i)^2
             f_i((x_i-ell'_i)/D'_i,x_(i+1)/D'_i)
                     +(1/2)Hub_(delta')(x_k-ell'_k).    (16)

This function is fixed before its run. Each term is convex and
nonnegative; F(0)=0 by(13). Every scaled local term is 1-smooth on its
two coordinates before multiplication by1/2. Their quadratic upper
bounds count each coordinate at most twice, including the last hinge.
Therefore F(x+w)<=F(x)+<grad F(x),w>+||w||^2/2. For convex F this implies

    F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>
                        >=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2. (17)

To prove it, apply the upper bound to V(z)=F(z)-<grad F(y),z> at x
with w=-grad V(x); y minimizes V. Adding(17) and its exchanged version
proves that grad F is 1-Lipschitz.

At the start of stage i the frozen coordinates are ell'_j+D'_j U'_j
for j<i, the current coordinate is ell'_i+D'_i, and later coordinates
are zero. During the actual local path x_i>=ell'_i and
0<=x_(i+1)<=ell'_(i+1). Each preceding component is in its post-checkpoint
zero wedge and each later component is in its pre-activation zero wedge.
They have zero gradient because they are differentiable nonnegative
functions at minima. The sole active term has coefficient1/2, so the
physical h_t exactly implements its local half-step h_t/2. At b_i the
next coordinate becomes ell'_(i+1)+D'_(i+1). This proves the state
induction through every original physical entry, including zeros.

In the tail the physical gradient is delta'e_k/2. Its terminal coordinate
above ell'_k is D'_k-delta's_k/2>=delta', so substitution gives

    2F(x_n)=(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)].                        (18)

For any actual samples a,b, including the terminal point and every
minimizer, define the directed residual
Q_ab=F(x_a)-F(x_b)+<grad F(x_a),x_b-x_a>
                           +||grad F(x_a)-grad F(x_b)||^2/2.
Equation(17), with x=x_b,y=x_a, gives Q_ab<=0 for every pair. Thus no
optimum, terminal, or remote interpolation row is omitted. The actual
vectors live in R^(k+1), so their Gram matrix is positive semidefinite.

At k=0 use F=Hub_(1/(1+s_0))/2 from1. Its closest radius is1 and its
payoff is1/[2(1+s_0)]. This is the zero-checkpoint member of the SAME
source family, rather than a different normalization.

## 5. Full source minimum set and a quantitative dual certificate

Because every summand of(16) is nonnegative and its envelope has zero
set(13), the COMPLETE minimum set is

    C_source={x:x_i<=zeta'_i,
                 epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta'_i-D'_i c'_(i,2), i<k;
                 x_k<=ell'_k},
    zeta'_i=ell'_i+D'_i c'_(i,1).                       (19)

It contains zero. If k>=1, e_0 violates its first horizontal constraint,
so 0<rho_S^2:=dist(e_0,C_source)^2<=1. Formula(19) retains every later
stage and the true terminal constraint; no fictitious cap on x_1 is added.

An exact all-row formula can also be recorded. Let A_rad have rows e_i^T
and(epsilon e_i-e_(i+1))^T for i<k, followed by e_k^T, with right side
t_rad from(19). For any independent row subset I, put
A_I=(A_rad)_I, r_I=A_I e_0-(t_rad)_I, K_I=A_I A_I^T,
lambda_I=K_I^(-1)r_I. Include the empty subset with value zero. Then

    rho_S^2=max_(I independent,lambda_I>=0) r_I^T K_I^(-1)r_I. (20)

For any feasible x and any nonnegative row weights lambda, completing
a square gives ||e_0-x||^2>=2lambda^T r_I-||A_I^T lambda||^2. This proves
each entry of(20) as a lower bound. At the true projection x_*, its
normal belongs to the cone of active rows: otherwise its residual from
projection on that closed finitely generated cone is a feasible small
displacement with positive inner product with e_0-x_*, contradicting
projection optimality. The cone is closed because nonnegative linear
representations can be reduced to independent subsets, a finite union
of closed injective images of orthants. Choose an independent active
representation. Activity gives r_I=K_I lambda, and equality in(20).
This is an identity, not an executed active-set calculation.

The needed quantitative certificate has NONZERO weights only on the
two first-stage rows, with all later weights zero. Let
z_bar=(c'_(0,1),c'_(0,2),0,...,0). Then

    w=e_0-z_bar=sigma'_0(epsilon+P_0,-1-Q_0,0,...)
      =sigma'_0(P_0-epsilon Q_0)e_0
         +sigma'_0(1+Q_0)(epsilon e_0-e_1).            (21)

Both coefficients are nonnegative by(9). Both first constraints are
equalities at z_bar. Therefore <w,x-z_bar><=0 for every x in the FULL
C_source, whether or not z_bar itself satisfies the later constraints.
Expanding ||e_0-x||^2 proves

    rho_S^2>=(sigma'_0)^2[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2]
           >=(sigma'_0)^2(s_0+2)^2/8.                 (22)

The last step uses(10) with s_loc=s_0/2. Later constraints can increase
the true distance relative to this bound. There is no assumption that
the first center remains globally feasible and no reuse of the false
one-source-stage extrapolation.

Combining the EXACT terminal value(18), the EXACT transfer product(15),
and the LOWER distance bound(22) gives the UPPER comparison

    E(F,h)<=Psi_S(h;selection),
    Psi_S= b_0^2/[(s_0+2)^2(1+s_k)]
                    *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[b_i/(Cs_i+2A+epsilon b_i)]^2.
                                                              (23)

The cancellation is D'_1=sigma'_0 b_0/2: the factors1/4 from its square,
1/2 from(18), and8 from(22) cancel exactly. Every later denominator in
(23) is an EXACT source transfer denominator, not a mass lower estimate.

## 6. Unchanged hierarchy and the minimum-level operation

Define r_h=c(c-2), v_h=a/(c-2)=beta/r_h,
M_j=1+S_j, d_j=a c^j, u_j=d_j/M_j, and M_max=M_(m-1).
Writing x=tau/m gives

    r_h=1+(4+2sqrt(2))x+lambda^2 x^2>1>beta,
    M_j=v_h c^j+(1-v_h)2^j,
    0<v_h<1, 1<=M_j<=c^j, a<=u_j<=c-2.               (24)

The mass formula follows by substitution in
M_(j+1)=2M_j+a c^j, with M_0=1. Its bounds use2<c and0<v_h<1.
Uniformly on(3),

    2<c<121/50, 1/4<a<=u_j<21/50<1/2,
    0<r_h-1<2/m.                                     (25)

For explicit checks, sqrt(2)<283/200 implies lambda<483/200, and
c<=lambda(1+1/6144)<484/200. Also beta>3/4 and c<3 imply a>1/4.
Finally4+2sqrt(2)<7, lambda^2<6 and m>=2 give
r_h-1<7/(4m)+3/(8m^2)<=31/(16m)<2/m.

Among any currently selected checkpoints choose one of minimum tree
level j. Its two complete H_j children contain only smaller levels, so
contain no currently selected checkpoint. Consequently its two actual
adjacent ordinary sums s,t satisfy

    s>=M_j-1, t>=M_j-1, b=1+d_j.                      (26)

This remains true for a first or last checkpoint and for tied minimum
levels. Removing it from the selected SUBSET reclassifies its full
physical b as ordinary, merging the adjacent sums to s+b+t. The word,
parameters, physical order and all remaining eligibility conditions stay
unchanged.

In the comparisons below write M=M_j, d=d_j, u=d/M. The operation is
performed on the proved upper functional Psi_S, not on actual states.
It claims neither monotonicity of the actual F under deletion nor
preservation of its backward P,Q. That distinction is justified by(23),
which already holds for every actual source instance before deletion.

## 7. All-count source upper comparison, including the first factor

Assume temporarily either

    (I) C>=r_h, or (II) A>=2C M_max.                   (27)

Section 8 proves that every parameter pair satisfies this dichotomy.
All arguments here use C>=1, A>=2C and0<u<=1/2.

FIRST SELECTED MINIMUM, with at least one selection after it.
Let R_next=2A+epsilon b_next. Cancelling the positive next numerator
and the identical remaining factors in(23), deletion is valid exactly
when

    b(s+b+t+2)<=(s+2)(Ct+R_next).                      (28)

The difference on the right increases with s,t,R_next: its derivatives
in s,t are Ct+R_next-b and C(s+2)-b, positive on(26) since
R_next>=4C, b=d+1<=M/2+1. Thus it suffices to set s=t=M-1.
Under(I), lower R_next to4C. The difference is exactly

    M^2[C-u(2+u)]+M[4C-2-2u]+3C-1.                   (29)

Since u<=c-2 gives u(2+u)<=r_h<=C, all three grouped terms are
nonnegative. Under(II), R_next>=4C M_max>=4CM, so the difference is
at least

    (M+1)C(5M-1)-(M/2+1)(5M/2+1)
      >=(15/4)M^2+M-2>0,                             (30)

using C>=1 and M>=1. This proves the new mixed first-factor deletion.

NONFIRST, NONLAST SELECTED MINIMUM.
Let Q_b=2A+epsilon b and R_next=2A+epsilon b_next. The exact required
inequality is

    b[C(s+b+t)+R_next]<=(Cs+Q_b)(Ct+R_next).           (31)

Its difference increases in Q_b,R_next,s,t: the nontrivial derivative
terms are Cs+Q_b-b and C(Ct+R_next-b), which are positive because
C(M-1)+4C-b>0. Lower s,t to M-1 and Q_b,R_next to2A. Put q=2A-C>=3C.
The resulting minimum difference is

    (CM+q)^2-(d+1)[C(2M+d)+q]
     =M^2[C^2-Cu(2+u)]
          +M[q(2C-u)-C(2+u)]+q^2-q.                 (32)

In(I) the first coefficient is nonnegative. The second is at least
C(6C-4u-2)>=2C, and q^2-q>=0. In(II),
q=2A-C>=C(4M_max-1)>=3CM, so z=q/M>=3C. Dividing the first
line of(32) by M^2 bounds it below by

    (C+z)^2-(3/2)[(5/2)C+z]
       >=16C^2-(33/4)C>0.                            (33)

Here(d+1)/M<=3/2, (2M+d)/M<=5/2, and the polynomial increases for
z>=3C,C>=1. This proves(31), with the full numerator b=d+1 retained.

LAST, NONFIRST SELECTED MINIMUM.
The ratio of the selected local factor to the deleted one is

    b^2(1+s+b+t)/[(Cs+2A+epsilon b)^2(1+t)]
       <=b^2(1+s+b+t)/[(s+4)^2(1+t)].                 (34)

The last expression decreases in t. Its s derivative, apart from its
positive factors, is2-s-2b-2t<0. Its maximum on(26) is therefore

    (d+1)^2(2M+d)/[(M+3)^2 M]
       <=(5/2)[(M/2+1)/(M+3)]^2<=5/8.               (35)

This case needs no choice between(I) and(II).

Repeat minimum-level deletion while there are at least two selections.
Each step is covered by(28),(31), or(34) and makes Psi_S nondecreasing.
There are finitely many selections, so the process reaches ONE eligible
checkpoint. It never requires defining a fictitious empty version of
the exceptional first factor. This is an induction, not an executed
recurrence or an enumeration of subsets.

For the remaining checkpoint put X=1+s>=M and Y=1+t>=M. Then
S_m=s+b+t=X+Y+d-1, and its upper ratio is

    Psi_S/H_base(H_m)
     =(d+1)^2(2X+2Y+2d-1)/[(X+1)^2Y].                (36)

Source eligibility(2) gives d>=9. The expression decreases with X and Y:
the X derivative has sign4-2X-4Y-4d<0 and the Y derivative has sign
-(2X+2d-1)<0. It is at most

    [(d+1)/(M+1)]^2(4+2u-1/M).

Since M>=9/u and0<u<21/50,

    (d+1)/(M+1)=u+(1-u)/(M+1)
        <=10u/(9+u)<70/157<9/20.

The last strict comparison is1400<1413. Also2+u<121/50. Thus

    Psi_S/H_base(H_m)<2(121/50)(9/20)^2=9801/10000.     (37)

Equations(23),(28)--(37) prove this same strict bound for EVERY
nonempty source selection, at arbitrary count, under(27). No lower
guarantee was used as an upper estimate.

## 8. Every independent parameter pair satisfies the source dichotomy

If C>=r_h then(I) holds. In the other case C<r_h, equations(1),(25)
give epsilon^2<C^2-1<4/m+4/m^2<=6/m<=1/256. Therefore epsilon<1/16 and

    theta_sp=pi/2-arctan epsilon>23/16>1,
    kappa theta_sp<=log C<log r_h<2/m,
    kappa<2/m<=1/768<1/16.                            (38)

The elementary inequalities pi>3 and arctan epsilon<epsilon justify
the first line; pi>3 follows from the inscribed regular hexagon.
The exponential branch for T is legitimate here because kappa<1 has
now been proved. Put X_sp=(1-kappa^2)theta_sp/kappa. Then

    X_sp>((1-kappa^2)theta_sp^2/2)m
         >(134895/131072)m>m.

The exact identity
1+(1+kappa)T=(exp(X_sp)-kappa)/(1-kappa)>=exp(X_sp) yields

    A>=C exp(X_sp)/epsilon>16C exp(m)>2C M_max.         (39)

For the last step, M_max<=c^(m-1) and exp(1)>5/2>c. This proves(II).
It covers all parameter scalings with m, including oscillatory choices.
For kappa>=1, the argument automatically stays in(I). No ordering of
kappa and epsilon is used, and epsilon=1/2 is included.

## 9. Main realization: all counts on the band C>=r_h

We rederive the actual main construction to state this additional scope
without treating the older unverified draft as a premise. Apply Section2
to the FULL physical ordinary blocks. In this section P_i,Q_i are their
full-step outputs. Set

    M_i^tr=P_i+max(epsilon b_i,epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon),
    sigma_i=1/M_i^tr, D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    D_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(b_i-1),
    ell_(i+1)=D_i sigma_i(1+Q_i).

Use(12) with b_loc=b_i, sigma=sigma_i, obtaining phi_i,c_(i,1),
c_(i,2),U_i,Y_i. Conditions(11) hold by definition. Define

    Phi_i(z)=D_i^2 phi_i((z_i-ell_i)/D_i,z_(i+1)/D_i), i<k,
    delta=D_k/(1+2s_k), Phi_k(z)=delta max(z_k-ell_k,0),
    J(z)=max_(0<=i<=k)Phi_i(z), f=env_1 J, x_0=e_0.    (40)

The local proximal points have second coordinate
sigma_i(1+Q_i-y_j+q_j) in[0,sigma_i(1+Q_i)]. Their first coordinate
minus U_i is sigma_i[epsilon(b_i-1)+u_j-p_j]>=0, by b_i>=B_sp and
the last estimates of Section2. Therefore at each proposed global
proximal point all preceding terms are in their post-checkpoint zero
wedges, the next has first local input at most0, and every further term
is in its pre-activation zero wedge. The current local subgradient is
an active subgradient of J, so it is the actual envelope gradient.

The physical state induction is the one with frozen coordinates
ell_j+D_j U_j, current coordinate ell_i+D_i, and later zeros.
After the checkpoint the new coordinate is
D_i sigma_i(Q_i+b_i)=ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1). In the tail, if u is its already
executed mass, the proposed proximal coordinate remains above ell_k
because D_k-(u+1)delta>=0 for0<=u<=s_k. Thus grad f=delta e_k there and

    2f(x_n)=D_k^2/(1+2s_k).                            (41)

Section3 proves convexity and1-smoothness. For every pair of actual
samples, write z_a=x_a-grad f(x_a). The exact residual is
Q_ab=J(z_a)-J(z_b)+<grad f(x_a),z_b-z_a><=0 by the subgradient
inequality. This includes every minimizer and the unqueried terminal.

The full minimum set is

    C_env={x:x_i<=zeta_i,
               epsilon x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon zeta_i-D_i c_(i,2), i<k;
               x_k<=ell_k}, zeta_i=ell_i+D_i c_(i,1).   (42)

Unlike the source case, z_bar=(c_(0,1),c_(0,2),0,...,0) is now feasible:
its second coordinate is ell_1, so the next local input is(0,0), and
all other terms vanish. The same normal representation(21), with
unprimed sigma and full-step outputs, proves it is the closest minimizer.
Consequently the EXACT main payoff is

    E(f,h)=(b_0-1)^2/
       {[(epsilon+P_0)^2+(1+Q_0)^2](1+2s_k)}
                     *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/M_i^tr]^2. (43)

Define A_floor=epsilon+epsilon^(-1) and C_floor=min(C,A_floor).
The function p(theta)+q(theta)/epsilon has minimum at an endpoint.
Indeed its derivative divided by R is
(epsilon^(-1)-kappa)cos theta-(1+kappa/epsilon)sin theta, which either
is always negative or changes sign once from positive to negative.
Thus every actual arc slope obeys p+q/epsilon>=C_floor, giving

    M_i^tr>=P_i+epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon
                      >=C_floor s_i+A_floor.           (44)

By(10),(43),(44) the actual main payoff has the upper surrogate

    Psi_M=2(b_0-1)^2/[(s_0+1)^2(1+2s_k)]
              *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/(C_floor s_i+A_floor)]^2.
                                                              (45)

Assume C>=r_h. Because A_floor>=5/2>r_h, this implies C_floor>=r_h;
also A_floor>=C_floor>=1. The same minimum-level operation works.
For a FIRST minimum, the exact required comparison is

    d(s+b+t+1)<=(s+1)(C_floor t+A_floor).

Its difference increases in s,t on(26). At s=t=M-1 it is
M^2[C_floor-u(2+u)]+M(A_floor-C_floor)>=0.
For an INTERIOR nonfirst minimum the comparison is

    d[C_floor(s+b+t)+A_floor]
                    <=(C_floor s+A_floor)(C_floor t+A_floor).

Its difference increases in s,t. At s=t=M-1, divided by M^2, it is
C_floor^2-C_floor u(2+u)+z(2C_floor-u)+z^2, with
z=(A_floor-C_floor)/M>=0, hence is nonnegative.
For a LAST nonfirst minimum, since C_floor s+A_floor>=1+s, the ratio
is at most d^2[1+2(s+b+t)]/[(1+s)^2(1+2t)]. It decreases in s,t and
at their minima is at most u^2(3+2u)<=1.

Stop again with one eligible checkpoint. Put X=1+s,Y=1+t, so X,Y>=M.
Its ratio to H_base is

    2d^2(2X+2Y+2d-1)/[X^2(2Y-1)].

It decreases in both X and Y, and hence is at most
2u^2[2+u+(1+u)/(2M-1)]. Main eligibility gives d>=4, so by(25)
M>200/21>19/2. The bracket is strictly less than
121/50+(71/50)/18=2249/900<5/2. Therefore

    E(f,H_m)<=Psi_M<(441/500)H_base(H_m), C>=r_h, k>=1. (46)

The main k=0 member is Hub_(1/(1+2S_m)) from1, with closest radius1
and payoff H_base(H_m).

For C<r_h, (39) controls the source's FIXED allowance A, whereas
M_i^tr in(43) uses the actual block-dependent Q_i. We do not assert
M_i^tr>=A. The following new argument derives a different sufficient
lower bound directly from the actual ordered full-step blocks.

## 9A. The remaining main band: discrete turning and adjacent levels

Throughout this section assume C<r_h. All statements are proved here
jointly with Sections 1--9; no new intermediate fact is presumed.
Write eta_turn=999/1000 and Delta_C=r_h-C>0. From (25),(38),

    epsilon<1/16, theta_sp>23/16,
    kappa<1/768, C<769/768,
    0<Delta_C<2/m<1/4.                                (46a)

Two slightly sharper elementary estimates will be useful:

    kappa*m*theta_sp < 44/25,  log c < 9/10.           (46b)

For the first, kappa*theta_sp<r_h-1 and the expansion in (24) gives
r_h-1<7/(4m)+3/(8m^2)<44/(25m); the last comparison follows from
m>=1536 and 44/25-7/4=1/100. For the second, c<121/50 and the positive
exponential series gives exp(9/10)>1+9/10+81/200+243/2000
=4853/2000>121/50. No numerical evaluation is used.

### A. An exact harmonic bound for each complete ordered child

Run the actual Section 2 backward construction on ONE complete H_j,
0<=j<=m, with the unchanged enclosing (m,tau). At a backward step
alpha, let y_mass=1+the sum of all physical steps strictly after it
within this H_j. This is a scalar mass, not the vertical coordinate
y_j of Section 2. If the step has tree level ell, its complete right
child H_ell lies strictly after it. Consequently y_mass>=M_ell and

    x_mass:=alpha/y_mass
       =(1+d_ell)/y_mass <= c-2+1/y_mass <3/2.        (46c)

Here d_ell<= (c-2)M_ell follows from (24). In reverse order y_mass
starts at 1 and increases to 1+S_j=M_j, with consecutive increments
equal to the actual alpha's. This bookkeeping retains their order.

For x>=0, log(1+x)>=2x/(2+x): their difference is zero at zero and
its derivative is x^2/[(1+x)(2+x)^2]>=0. Thus, if
ell_mass=log(1+x_mass), (46c) gives

    x_mass <= (1+x_mass/2)ell_mass
            <= (c/2+1/(2*y_mass))ell_mass.

Also log(1+x_mass)<=x_mass and x_mass<3/2 imply

    ell_mass/y_mass
       <=x_mass/y_mass
       =(1+x_mass)[1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha)]
       <=(5/2)[1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha)].

Both sums now telescope in the actual reverse order. Define
Hharm_j=sum_(steps in H_j) alpha/y_mass. Then

    Hharm_j <= (c/2)log M_j+5/4.                      (46d)

The empty H_0 has Hharm_0=0 and is included. This is a symbolic finite
sum inequality, not an executed recurrence or a continuous flow.

### B. A bound on every DISCRETE angle, with caps included

At a backward step in this same H_j, write the PRE-update state in
polar form r=rho_loc*(cos phi_pol,-sin phi_pol). The actual projected
slope that is about to be added is v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta). Put
gamma_step=phi_pol-theta. Section 2 gives
0<=gamma_step<pi/2: before a cap, phi_pol is at least the previous
projected angle, and the new projected angle is no larger; at a cap
theta=0. These observations include a zero step.

In the uncapped case, equation (6) is exactly

    sin gamma_step
       =kappa*cos gamma_step
           +kappa*(alpha-1)*R(theta)/rho_loc.

In the capped case the cap test (5) gives the SAME relation with <=
in place of equality and R(theta)=C. Therefore, in BOTH cases,

    sin gamma_step <= kappa*[1+alpha*C/rho_loc].      (46e)

Before using a small-angle estimate, (10) already gives the unconditional
radius bound rho_loc>=y_mass/sqrt(2). Combine it with (46c), C<2,
sqrt(2)<3/2, and kappa<1/768 to get

    sin gamma_step < (11/2)*kappa <11/1536<1/100.

Because gamma_step is in [0,pi/2), this proves
cos gamma_step>eta_turn. In particular, projecting the exact vector
update r+alpha*v(theta) onto r/rho_loc gives

    rho_new >=rho_loc+alpha*R(theta)*cos gamma_step
             >=rho_loc+eta_turn*alpha.

The initial endpoint radius is sqrt(1+epsilon^2)>=1. Induction over
these exact additions therefore improves the PRE-update bound to

    rho_loc>=eta_turn*y_mass.                         (46f)

No uncapped hypothesis was used in this induction.

Let delta_pol=phi_pol-phi_new>=0 be the polar-angle change in this
single update, and let z_step=alpha*R(theta)/rho_loc. Resolving the
two vectors along the old radial and tangent directions gives the
EXACT identity

    tan delta_pol=z_step*sin gamma_step /
                              (1+z_step*cos gamma_step).

In either branch the stronger relation preceding (46e) implies
sin gamma_step<=kappa*(1+z_step). The denominator is at least
cos gamma_step*(1+z_step). Since delta_pol<=tan delta_pol,

    delta_pol <= kappa*z_step/cos gamma_step
       <= [C/(eta_turn^2)]*kappa*alpha/y_mass
       <= (101/100)*kappa*alpha/y_mass.               (46g)

The last rational bound follows from C<769/768<501/500 and
(501/500)*(1000/999)^2<101/100. Thus (46g) retains the complete
discrete error at each step, even if that step were to cap.

For an uncapped POST-update state, representation (7) gives
0<=phi_new-theta=arctan(kappa*nu/(1+nu))<=kappa. For a capped state,
the cap calculation in Section 2 gives y_new<=kappa*(u_new-C), so
phi_new<=arctan kappa<=kappa; the SAME lower bound
theta>=phi_new-kappa holds. Summing (46g) from the endpoint and
using (46d), every projected angle, including a possible cap, satisfies

    theta >=theta_sp-kappa*[(101/100)Hharm_j+1]
      > theta_sp-(44/(25*theta_sp))*(111/100)
      = theta_sp-1221/(625*theta_sp) >1/16.           (46h)

For the middle inequality, M_j<=c^j, j<=m, (46b), and c<121/50 show

    [(101/100)Hharm_j+1]/m
       <=(101/100)*[1089/1000+5/(4m)]+1/m <111/100.

The final rational inequality holds at theta_sp>23/16 because
theta_sp*(theta_sp-1/16)>(23/16)*(22/16)=253/128>1221/625.
This proves (46h) without assuming in advance that caps are absent;
it now excludes them on complete H_j. The endpoint angle itself also
satisfies (46h). Nothing here replaces the discrete rule by a flow.

Since R(theta)>=1 and theta>1/16, every actually used slope on H_j
has q(theta)>=sin(1/16)>1/32. The last inequality follows by integrating
cos t>=1-t^2/2>1/2 for 0<=t<=1/16. Using p+q>=1 and epsilon<1/16,

    p+q/epsilon =p+q+(epsilon^(-1)-1)q >47/32.

Consequently the actual output of this complete ordered child obeys

    P(H_j)+Q(H_j)/epsilon-C*S_j >= S_j/4.             (46i)

For nonempty H_j this is strict, since 47/32-C>1/4 by (46a).
For H_0 all three outputs vanish and the weak inequality is exact.

### C. Transfer the surplus to arbitrary incoming blocks

For any selection, write lev_i for the tree level of its i-th selected
checkpoint. For i>=1 set j_i=min(lev_(i-1),lev_i). The ACTUAL ordinary
block_i ends with a complete H_(j_i), without a selected point inside
that trailing child. Here is a direct combinatorial proof. Number the
physical positions from zero. Induction on the word definition shows
that the level at position t is the exponent of 2 in t+1. Positions of
level at least j_i have t+1 divisible by 2^(j_i); any two distinct such
positions differ by at least 2^(j_i). The final 2^(j_i)-1 positions
before a checkpoint of level at least j_i have precisely the word
H_(j_i), with the same enclosing parameters. The previous selection
lies at or before the position preceding this child. This proves the
claim also when j_i=0, when the child is empty.

The Section 2 backward algorithm processes this suffix FIRST from the
same endpoint (epsilon,-1). Its outputs on that suffix are therefore
exactly those in (46i). Additional earlier steps may cap and may have
different angles. For them (44) still proves p+q/epsilon>=C, because
C<A_floor in this band. Hence their contribution to the surplus beyond
C times physical mass is nonnegative. Keeping the actual full outputs,

    P_i+Q_i/epsilon-C*s_i >= S_(j_i)/4.

Now A_floor=epsilon+epsilon^(-1)>16 and C<2, so A_floor-C>1/4.
The definition of the ACTUAL main transfer denominator gives

    M_i^tr >= P_i+epsilon+(1+Q_i)/epsilon
       >= C*(s_i+1)+(S_(j_i)+1)/4
       >= C*(s_i+1)+Delta_C*M_(j_i) =: D_i^cmp,
                                          1<=i<k.  (46j)

This proves the required lower bound for every count and every pair
of adjacent selected levels. No assertion M_i^tr>=A is made. The
max(epsilon*b_i,...) in M_i^tr remains unchanged and only makes this
lower bound stronger. The exceptional first radius remains exactly
the one in (43), bounded below only by (10).

It follows from the EXACT actual payoff (43) that

    E(f,H_m) <= Psi_low,
    Psi_low = 2*(b_0-1)^2 /[(s_0+1)^2*(1+2*s_k)]
             *product_(i=1)^(k-1)[(b_i-1)/D_i^cmp]^2. (46k)

This upper functional depends on adjacent selected levels as well as
block sums. Its denominators are comparison quantities; after a
deletion they are recalculated by their definition, never identified
with old or new actual P,Q.

### D. All-count deletion of this upper functional

Choose a globally minimum selected level j, while k>=2. Set M=M_j,
d=d_j, u=d/M, b=1+d. Its adjacent sums s,t satisfy (26). Write
X=1+s>=M and Y=1+t>=M. The merged incoming mass plus one is

    1+s+b+t=X+Y+d.

FIRST SELECTED MINIMUM. The next denominator is C*Y+Delta_C*M,
since the minimum of the first two selected levels is j. Cancellation
in (46k) shows that deleting this first selection increases Psi_low if

    d*(X+Y+d) <= X*(C*Y+Delta_C*M).                  (46l)

The right side minus the left increases in X,Y: its respective
derivatives are C*Y+Delta_C*M-d>0 and C*X-d>0 on X,Y>=M,
because C>=1>u. At X=Y=M its value is
M^2*[r_h-u*(2+u)]>=0 by u<=c-2. This proves (46l).

NONFIRST, NONLAST SELECTED MINIMUM. Both its incoming denominator and
the next one use the same M_j, and thus equal C*X+Delta_C*M and
C*Y+Delta_C*M. After deletion the new adjacent-level child is contained
in the new incoming block, by Section 9A.C. Its mass plus one is at
most X+Y+d. Therefore the NEW comparison denominator is at most

    C*(X+Y+d)+Delta_C*(X+Y+d)=r_h*(X+Y+d).

It is enough to prove

    d*r_h*(X+Y+d)
       <=(C*X+Delta_C*M)*(C*Y+Delta_C*M).            (46m)

The difference increases in X,Y: its derivative in X is at least
C*(C+Delta_C)*M-r_h*d=r_h*M*(C-u)>0, and the Y derivative is the
same with X,Y exchanged. At X=Y=M the difference is

    r_h*M^2*[r_h-u*(2+u)]>=0.

This proves (46m), including the possible increase in the new child
level. No false conservation of the actual block outputs occurs.

LAST, NONFIRST SELECTED MINIMUM. Here D_i^cmp>=C*(s+1)>=s+1.
The local selected-to-deleted ratio in (46k) is at most

    d^2*[1+2*(s+b+t)]/[(s+1)^2*(1+2*t)].

It decreases in s,t and at s=t=M-1 equals
u^2*[(4+2*u)*M-1]/(2*M-1)<=u^2*(3+2*u)<=1.
For the first inequality the cross-multiplied difference is
2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. For the second, 0<u<=1/2 and the function
u^2*(3+2*u) increases to 1 at u=1/2. This proves the terminal case.

Repeat until a single eligible checkpoint remains. Every remaining
subset defines its own valid Psi_low, so (46l)--(46m) and the terminal
case apply at each deletion, including ties in the minimum level.
The physical word and enclosing parameters remain unchanged. At one
checkpoint (46k) is exactly the scalar one-checkpoint upper expression
used after (45). Its calculation there uses only d>=4, u<21/50 and
the full prefix/suffix child bounds, not C>=r_h. Thus

    E(f,H_m)<(441/500)*H_base(H_m), C<r_h, k>=1.      (46n)

Together with (46), this proves the same main-family bound for ALL
independent kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2 and every main-eligible selection.
The equality C=r_h belongs to Section 9. Every other eligibility
endpoint is retained by the unchanged B_sp condition. The estimates
are finite and uniform: no regularity, rate, or limiting value is
assumed for the choice of epsilon or kappa as m varies.

## 10. Analytical boundary tests on the actual functions

The two prescribed tests are reproduced to check the normalization and
chronology of the new comparison, not used as accepted premises.

TWO SOURCE CHECKPOINTS, ZERO ORDINARY STEPS.
Set epsilon=kappa=1/2, b_0=b_1=10 and all ordinary blocks empty or zero.
Put sigma=1/(A+5/2); from(1), A>5/2 and0<sigma<1/5. The local function is

    phi(z)=sigma max{0,C(z_0-1+sigma/2),
                         (z_0-1+sigma/2)/2-z_1+sigma},
    f_loc=env_1 phi.

The full original source function on R^3 is

    F(x)=f_loc(x_0,x_1)/2
          +(25sigma^2/2)f_loc(x_1/(5sigma),x_2/(5sigma))
          +Hub_(25sigma^2)(x_2)/2.                     (47)

Its full minimum set is both scaled wedges(13) together with x_2<=0.
The point x_*=(1-sigma/2,sigma,0) is feasible. At its second local
input(1/5,0), the horizontal plane is negative and the endpoint plane
is-2/5+5sigma/4<-3/20. Its first endpoint normal is
e_0-x_*=sigma(1/2,-1,0), so it is the closest minimizer and

    rho_S^2=5sigma^2/4.

The complete actual queried states and gradients are

    x_0=e_0, g_0=(sigma/4,-sigma/2,0),
    x_1=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma,0),
    g_1=(0,5sigma^2/4,-5sigma^2/2),
    x_2=(1-5sigma/2,5sigma-25sigma^2/2,25sigma^2).

Both local terms vanish at x_2; F(x_2)=(25sigma^2)^2/4. The true payoff
is250sigma^2. The new upper formula(23) gives625sigma^2, in the correct
direction. It does not give the false50sigma^2 from extrapolating the
one-stage radius25sigma^2/4. The polyhedron keeps the terminal cap on
x_2, never inserts x_1<=0, and(21) is valid for it. This is outside(3),
so no hierarchy deletion or family escape is inferred from this test.

EQUAL MASS, DIFFERENT ORDERED OUTPUTS.
Keep epsilon=kappa=1/2. Set theta_*=arctan(3/4),
R_*=C exp(-theta_*/2), p_*=4R_*/5,q_*=3R_*/5,
A_*=1+2(1+q_*)/C. At backward endpoint(1/2,-1), a last local step1
fails(5), and(6) becomes2sin theta-(3/2)cos theta=0. Its actual root is
theta_*, with normal n(theta_*)=(1/2,-1). The local word(A_*,1) has
backward states

    r_2=(1/2,-1), r_1=(1/2+p_*,-1-q_*),
    r_0=(1/2+p_*+A_*C,-1-q_*),

and projections v_2=(1/2,-1),v_1=(p_*,-q_*),v_0=(C,0). The cap test
at A_* has right side1+q_*+1/4+p_*/2>1+q_*. Hence its actual outputs
are P=A_*C+p_*,Q=q_*>0.

For local word(1,A_*), the last A_* caps because A_*>1+3/(2C), and the
earlier step1 stays capped. Its states are

    r_2=(1/2,-1), r_1=(1/2+A_*C,-1),
    r_0=(1/2+(A_*+1)C,-1),

with v_1=v_0=(C,0), and its outputs are P=(A_*+1)C,Q=0. Both local
sums equal A_*+1. For the source functions these are the physical
ordinary words(2A_*,2) and(2,2A_*). Add any source-eligible checkpoint
and tail: equations(12),(14)--(19) explicitly give their full static
functions, physical trajectories, and entire minimum sets. Formula(23)
continues to hold for EACH distinct output, using only the proved
inequality P+Q>=A_*+1. Neither the initial bound nor its scalar pruning
claims equality of these outputs.

For a zero local step(6) retains the current point and contributes
zero to P,Q. For a single arbitrarily huge local step alpha satisfying
alpha>=1+(1/kappa-epsilon)/C, (5) caps immediately, giving
P=C alpha,Q=0. Both(10) and(44) retain their indicated directions.
Thus the projection bounds handle the zero and huge-step endpoints
without a continuous-time replacement. No numerical evaluation of any
of these trajectories was made.

## 11. The original benchmark, read from accepted evidence

Only fact_id04219554e41d9750 supplies the following benchmark inputs.
For x,y>=0 let Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2 and
G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2. Let C_0=B_0=1 and, for n>=1,

    C_n=max_(0<=j<n)Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n)G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

These are the ORIGINAL ALL-split recurrences. The accepted fact gives
the positive limit Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1), and for every j>=0

    lambda^(-j)B_(2^j-1)<=Z_1
          <=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(2^j))+3/100)^2.          (48)

Its Section8 certifies every row of the public fixed interval table
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
The header, row1024 and matching hash were read in this round. No
generator, recurrence, checker or mathematical evaluation was executed.
With D80=1208925819614629174706176 that accepted saved row gives

    D80 B_1024<=19229705732238461978760749948
              <15910 D80=19234009790068750169575260160.

Since sqrt(2)>140/99 and lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2)>6725 (the numerator
slack is82), and sqrt(15910)<127, (48) at j=10 gives

    Z_1<(15910+381/50+9/10000)/6725
         <15918/6725<237/100.                          (49)

These are written scalar deductions from existing accepted evidence.

For the physical hierarchy(24), v_h>1-9/(4m)>255/256 and
lambda^(-m)<1/256. Indeed r_h-beta<9/(4m) follows by expanding(24),
using tau<=1/4, lambda^2<6 and m>=2. Consequently

    (1+2S_m)/lambda^m
       =2v_h(1+tau/m)^m+2(1-v_h)(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m). (50)

Binomial truncation gives(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4 when m>=2.
At tau=1/4, (50) is strictly greater than
2(255/256)(81/64)-1/256=20623/8192>5/2. Uniformly for
1/5<=tau<=1/4 it is greater than
2(255/256)(121/100)-1/256=6161/2560>12/5. Thus

    B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,
    B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,tau))<79/80, 1/5<=tau<=1/4.      (51)

For each fixed0<tau<=1/4, v_h tends1, (1+tau/m)^m tends exp(tau),
and(2/lambda)^m tends0. Combining(48),(50) proves

    lim_m B_(n_m)H_base(H_(m,tau))=Z_1/(2exp(tau)).      (52)

No parameter supremum is interchanged with a limit in this argument.

## 12. Complete joint theorem for both actual families

THEOREM (joint unverified analytical candidate).
Let the physical family be exactly(3), the source functions exactly
(12),(14)--(16), and the main functions exactly(40). For every real
0<tau<=1/4, integer m>=1536, every independent kappa>0 and0<epsilon<=1/2,
and EVERY nonempty chronological selection satisfying its source
threshold(2), at any count1<=k<=n_m,

    2F(x_(n_m))/dist(e_0,argmin F)^2
                         <(9801/10000)/(1+2S_m).       (53)

For every such m,tau, EVERY independent kappa>0 and 0<epsilon<=1/2,
and EVERY nonempty main-eligible selection, also at any count,

    2f(x_(n_m))/dist(e_0,argmin f)^2
                         <(441/500)/(1+2S_m).          (54)

All minima are zero. Every physical trajectory, complete minimum set,
smoothness normalization and original interpolation row is justified
in this SAME joint proof. The exact main payoff is (43), using full
ordered ordinary-block outputs and the true closest radius. The source
payoff is (18) divided by the squared distance to the full minimum polyhedron
(19), with the valid lower distance certificate (22). Neither actual
family is replaced by a reduced lower guarantee. The parameter endpoints
and all horizon-dependent choices are included. Multiplying either
function by L>0 and using physical steps h_t/L gives the same payoffs.

Define L_source,all to be the supremum of the ACTUAL closest-distance
payoffs of all source functions, over all their independent parameters
and eligible selections, including their k=0 member. Then the exact
source-only optimum is

    L_source,all(H_(m,tau))=1/[2(1+S_m)].               (55a)

Indeed M_m>=2^m>=32, from(24). The k=0 payoff divided by H_base equals
1-1/(2M_m)>=63/64>9801/10000, since630000>627264. Thus(53) puts every
nonempty source selection strictly below that actual k=0 member.

Define L_source,all^H=max{H_base,L_source,all}. Then

    L_source,all^H(H_(m,tau))=H_base(H_(m,tau)).          (55)

Define L_main,all as the supremum over every actual main function (40),
all its allowed parameters and eligible selections, including k=0.
Define L_both,all as the supremum over the union of these main and
source families, keeping each family's own exact definition. Then

    L_main,all(H_(m,tau))=L_both,all(H_(m,tau))
                       =H_base(H_(m,tau))=1/(1+2*S_m). (56)

The main k=0 Huber member attains these suprema; the source k=0 value
1/[2(1+S_m)] is strictly smaller, and (53),(54) bound all nonempty
selections strictly below Huber. There is no mixed product constructed
from factors of the two different realizations.

For EVERY integer m>=1536 the original all-split benchmark therefore
satisfies

    B_(n_m)*L_both,all(H_(m,1/4))<237/250,
    B_(n_m)*L_both,all(H_(m,tau))<79/80,
                                      1/5<=tau<=1/4. (57)

The same finite strict bounds hold for L_main,all, L_source,all and
L_source,all^H. For each fixed 0<tau<=1/4, all four quantities have
the exact B-scaled limit

    Z_1/(2*exp(tau)).                                 (58)

For L_source,all this uses its ratio to H_base tending to one, since
M_m>=2^m tends infinity. For the other quantities it is exactly (52).
No parameter supremum is interchanged with a limit.

PROOF. Sections2--5 establish the actual source upper comparison(23),
with the full source minimum polyhedron retained. Sections6--8 prove
its all-count bound(37) uniformly over every pair, giving(53). Section9
proves the actual main upper comparison and its all-count bound for
C>=r_h. Section 9A pays the remaining band C<r_h with a discrete
angle estimate, a trailing-child surplus for the actual outputs, and
minimum-level deletion of the resulting upper comparison. These give
(54) in the entire parameter domain. The k=0 values and (51),(52)
give (55)--(58). Every supporting calculation is included jointly,
without adopting an unverified intermediate result as an accepted fact.

This theorem is an upper comparison for exactly the two specified
actual families, at their advertised smoothness. It is not an upper
bound on the unrestricted W_n(H_m). It makes no assertion for tau>1/4,
does not contradict the accepted original tau=1 lower witness
5b7ff4253c38b90a, and does not resolve the original all-word exact-profile
minimax problem. All new mathematics here is UNVERIFIED. There is no
new accepted fact or claim of original-target completion.

# Signed interval certificates: exact matching and a complete equal-tail obstruction

Status: COMPLETE JOINT RESEARCH DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4 round43,
the first of two analytical rounds allocated after completed42 by
TASK.md and master_guidance c3f16cd4617d4d93. No numerical evaluation,
solver, machine algebra, replay, verifier, consult or subagent was used.
The original seed and the original exact-profile Goal remain OPEN.

Accepted mathematical inputs are fact04219554e41d9750, Sections10--11,
for the original primitive identity and its full-space composition, and
fact1252b8823bb5a290 for the interpolation convention used there.
All new ingredients below are proved jointly; no unverified finding is
used as a correctness premise.

The variable-step interval identity is NOT new: it was already derived
in shared cd0f93f08a50828f. Its source inspiration is Jinze Zhao,
*Constant Steps Are s-Composable: An Exact Interpolation Certificate for
Gradient Descent*, arXiv:2609.11784v1, Sections3.1--3.2. That exact local
text and its interval proof were read. No constant-step positivity theorem
is imported. Sibling8a77ec65ac2eeb52 also correctly warned that homogeneous
interval diagonalization does not solve a changing-word objective problem.

The new outcomes here are:

* an explicit, unique solution of the seven signed suffix/adjacent mode
  equations, with all twenty original multipliers displayed;
* a full original-array obstruction for EVERY word h=(u,w,t,t)>=0 in
  that construction family at sum h>=168/25;
* a nonempty dense algebraic calibration with every multiplier positive
  and the entire five-dimensional remainder zero;
* an explicit valid ConPP word at which this seven-mode representation
  forces Lambda12<0, and a polynomial twelve-edge skew correction that
  repairs that exact calibration while preserving the whole matrix.

The equal-tail restriction belongs to the chosen construction family.
Neither equality of those steps nor the seven-mode restriction nor a
zero remainder is imposed on the original cone. The broader seven-mode
family with unequal last steps is NOT decided here.

## 1. Original full-space identity and elementary boundary consequences

For h=(h0,h1,h2,h3)>=0 put s=sum h_i, C=1+s, and
P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j. Indices of all five independent formal gradients are
0,1,2,3,4. Let e be the column of five ones, and use the original matrix

    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,
    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,
    D=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)).

For a zero-diagonal array Lambda, let L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda and
M=D+sym(LT), where sym(A)=(A+A^T)/2. The original sufficient condition
is Lambda_ij>=0 for all i!=j, L^T e=omega, and M PSD on ALL R5.
The accepted identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g0,...,g4)>,        (1)
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    x_i=x0-sum_(j<i)h_j g_j,
    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                             -s(s+1)||g4||^2.

Actual smooth convex trajectories satisfy every Q_ij<=0. The sign
orientation and the factor in (1) are the ones in accepted04219554e41d9750.

Every correct flow has e^T M e=0. Indeed Te=e+P,
2sum h_i P_i=s^2-sum h_i^2,
e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2, and omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2.
PSD therefore forces Me=0, by evaluating (e+az)^T M(e+az) for both
signs of small a. Under the flow this gives

    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i), i<4,
    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                         (2)

For example, (Me)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(LP)_i/2 at i<4, while
(Me)_4=-sC+(LP)_4/2. These expansions also prove (2) directly.

For s>0 both endpoint steps obey

    h0,h3 <= 2C/(C+1)<2.                                  (3)

For h0>1, (2) gives 2h0<=s out0, and the first diagonal gives
h0(2-h0)>=(h0-1)out0. Combining proves the first inequality;
h0<=1 is already below the bound. For h3>0, the terminal diagonal
gives out4>=s(s+2), and (2) gives
2sC>=h3 out4>=h3 s(s+2). The zero case needs no division.
Here out_i=sum_(j!=i)Lambda_ij denotes the outgoing mass. The same
proofs apply verbatim to the compressed word at any smaller length.

We will use the stronger construction requirement M=0. For any queried
index i its diagonal is

    M_ii=h_i(2-h_i)+sum_(j<i)Lambda_ij
                                  +(1-h_i)sum_(j>i)Lambda_ij. (4)

Thus a positive h_i<=1 is impossible when all Lambda>=0 and M=0.
If h_i=0, (4) forces every outgoing coefficient at i to vanish;
the zero flow then forces all incoming coefficients to vanish. Such a
vertex is isolated. Removing it compresses the zero physical step and
restricts the full identity without identifying two formal gradients.
Conversely no compression is used to justify an untested original row.

At the threshold no zero step is possible in this zero-remainder class.
Here is a bound sufficient to prove that assertion. For a positive bridge
step 1+delta with prefix capacity A and suffix capacity E, put
eta=delta/E>0. Use the scalar function with derivative
clamp(x,[-eta,1]), starting from x0=A. At the bridge query x=1;
the bridge lands at -delta, and the tail ends at -eta, with all its
gradients -eta. For this function f(x)=x g-g^2/2. Direct substitution
and the GD telescope give V_h=x0^2-(x_n+s g_n)^2=A^2-C^2 eta^2.
Its derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz, so it is
an actual convex 1-smooth function; normalize f(0)=0.
Consequently every feasible array requires

    C delta <= A E.                                       (5)

With at most two positive steps, (3) on the compressed word gives s<4.
With three positive steps, write the middle one as 1+delta. Both
endpoints are <2, so A,E<3. Equation (5) gives delta<9/C, hence
C<6+9/C, or C<3+3sqrt(2)<15/2<193/25. The all-zero case is immediate.
This proves the asserted zero-step exclusion without dropping a formal
coordinate from an allegedly feasible original array.

Define p0=1 and p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. Then Tp=e and Le=0. Hence

    p^T M p=p^T Dp=1-C^2 p4^2,                             (6)

where the last equality telescopes
h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2. When M=0 this is an equality
C|p4|=1, rather than merely a necessary inequality. In particular,
after all four positive steps have been shown >1, put r_i=h_i-1>0;
then
